现在，我专门进入了一个带有“push back”标签的会话。额，这具体记录了每一次工程师反驳代理的情况。最近有这么一件事，我想是 Anthropic 那边，或者说是传言吧，我不知道这到底有没有事实依据，但传闻说 Anthropic 正在抵制……如果你对代理不礼貌，你可能会失去账号或被封禁。但我特别发现，在反驳这方面，当我专门去查看 Matt 写的 Rust 代码或 Go 代码时，每当他提出反驳，这就表明他掌握着代理所不知道的知识。

I don't know if this is actually grounded by any any facts, but uh Anthropic was pushing back on uh if you're being rude to the agent, you could potentially lose your account or get blocked. Um but what I find specifically when push back um when I'm specifically looking for any Rust code or Go code that Matt has written uh and whenever he pushes back, it's a good signal that he has knowledge that the agent does not.
</details>

这种情况通常表现为：尽管 Rust 编译器能编译通过，但我们决定以某种特定方式构建 API。我们已经决定了代码应该如何组织，以及该代码应该如何与代码库的不同部分进行交互。此外，反驳还包括指出诸如重复实现某些服务的情况，而代理可能并不了解相关的上下文。还有很多其他原因，但我真正想表达的是，作为所谓的“初级”Rust 工程师，我没有足够的背景知识来向代理提出反驳，去对它说：“嘿，你这里做错了，或者你做的选择很奇怪。”我通常会对代理用 Rust 写的任何东西全盘照收。

<details>
<summary>Original English</summary>
And this usually comes in the form of we have decided our our API is structured certain way despite the fact that the Rust code compiler compiles. We have made decisions on how that code should be set up and how that code should interact with different parts of the codebase. Also pushing back on things like duplicating uh services that maybe the agent didn't have context around. There's a lot of other like a bunch of reasons, but what I'm getting at, me as like quoteunquote a junior Rust engineer, uh I don't have enough context to be able to push back to the agent to say, "Hey, you're doing this wrong or you're making like weird choices." I usually take everything that the agent writes in Rust for face value.
</details>

这就导致了当我发起 PR 时，在代码审查阶段会受到人类工程师的大量反驳。这主要是因为大家知道我不写 Rust 代码。对于代码库，甚至在审查的时候，我能够提供的反驳并不多。所以当代码送到 Gretile 那里时，即使是五星好评的审查意见，我也就直接放行了。那么，我该如何避免这种反驳呢？我所做的实际上是开始收集每一个这样的标签和跟踪数据。然后我要指出的另一点是，控制台本身捕获了每一个会话，我拥有所有的具体细节。这实际上只是它的命令行版本。我一直在做的就是捕获所有这些会话，然后放入一个打好标签的数据集中。

<details>
<summary>Original English</summary>
And what that ends up turning into is when I open up PR, I get a lot of push back from the engineers, the human engineers at review time. Uh this is mainly because we know I don't write Rust code. There's not a lot of push back I can provide to the codebase. um or even at the time of review. So by the time it gets to Gretile, it's a five out of five review. I just let it slide. Um so how do I prevent that push back? What I've done is actually started collecting every single one of these labels and the traces. Uh and then one thing I'll point out is that the the console itself, I capture every single session. I got all the uh specific details. This is actually just a command line version of that. Uh what I've been doing is I've been capturing all these sessions and then dropping into a data set that's been labeled.
</details>

这样一来，我现在就可以直接运行一个名为 twin review 的小型命令行工具。我会指定一个 PR 编号，比如 381，然后运行它，它就会真正去遍历并从本质上审查代码，就像是 Matt 在审查一样。我现在实现这一点的方式就是捕获跟踪数据。我创建了数据集，并且也做了一些后训练（post training）。这里你可以自由选择实现方式，但我专门针对 Matt 在 Go 和 Rust 代码中提出的每一次反驳做了一些后训练。我让它在我的 GitHub PR 上以审查者 Matt 的身份为我提供评论。

Uh so now I can just like run a little command line tool called twin review. Um and I'll grab a a PR number 381 and I'll run that and it'll actually run through and essentially review the code as if it was Matt. Now the way I'm doing this is I'm capturing the traces. I created the data set. I've also done some post training. Uh and this is like where you know choose your own adventure but I've done some post training uh specifically on every time Matt pushes back within Go Rus code. Uh and I I asked for it to provide me comments as a viewer Matt on my GitHub PR.
</details>

所以，这些数据完全只存在于跟踪记录中，并没有采用任何 GitHub PR 的审查数据。我认为大家可能也可以采用那种模式。但我发现的是，我们团队的大多数人已经开始在命令行上审查代码了。你会让 Claude 去审查代码，或者让 Pi harness 去审查代码，或者你去审查这个 PR，但你不会在 GitHub 本身上面进行大量的反驳或撰写大量评论。虽然我们现在所看到的是，审查代码的方式正在转变——以前代码专门由人类在 GitHub 上审查，现在则是由代理审查；而现在，代码变成了完全在你的 harness 中进行审查，没有任何监督、评论，或者任何直接导向你们共享代码库的内容。

So uh this is all data that's only specifically within the traces. So this is not taking any GitHub PR review data. Uh which I think you potentially could also follow that pattern. So you'll have Claude review the code or the Pi harness review code um or you review this PR but you don't do a lot of push back or a lot of commentary on GitHub itself. Though what we're seeing right now, we're shifting from code being reviewed specifically on GitHub by agents. Well, previously humans and now agents to now code is specifically this getting reviewed in your harness without any sort of oversight or commentary or anything that sort of navigates directly to your um your shared codebase.
</details>

### 专用微调与直接偏好优化

还有就是做专用微调（specialized fine-tuning）或者直接偏好优化（direct preference optimization）。后者会需要多得多的算力，或者更大的、某些大型硬件来完成。所以大概会选专用微调。呃，如果你了解或者说这就是我做的方式，我是在像 5090 这样较小的 GPU 上对其进行量化。呃，如果你不了解专用微调，这就像是在书的页边写批注。如果你的书就是你的模型，那么页边的批注就是你的权重，呃，或者更确切地说是你的适配器（adapters）。也就是说，在已经预训练好的模型之上生成的那些权重。很好。正如大家所见，我是在一个 Quinton 4B 模型上完成这一步的。呃，效果挺不错的。呃，我确实试过在一些更大的模型上做这个。呃，特别是偏好优化，耗费了大量的算力。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
is also you do specialized fine tuning or direct preference optimization. The latter is going to take a lot more compute or larger some large hardware to do that. So probably specialized fine tuning. Uh if you know about quick or if that's how I'm doing this I'm quantizing it on smaller GPUs like a 5090. Uh, and if you don't know about specialized fine-tuning, this is like writing notes in the margin of a book. If your book is your model, the notes in the margin are your weights, uh, or your adapters rather. So, the weights you generate on top of the the the model that's already been pre-trained. Cool. And I did this as as you saw on a Quinton 4B model. Uh, it works pretty good. Uh, I did try to do this on some larger models. Uh, so specifically for preference optimization, it took a lot of compute.
</details>

### VLM Serving 配置优化智能体

好的。大家好。我的名字是 Rohan，这是关于最近构建出来的优化智能体的一个快速演示。先快速介绍一下这个智能体的目的是什么、目标是什么，然后我将进入简短的演示，以及我们刚才运行的一次前期运行的结果。我们这里的目标是构建一个智能体，帮助针对任意给定的模型找到最佳的 VLM 服务配置。呃，目的是减少手动寻找最佳配置和 VLM 服务方案（recipe）所花费的时间。如果我们能将其自动化，让它在后台运行，并且让智能体更快地找到方案和优化参数，那么它将节省大量时间。这就是该智能体的目标，接下来详细说明一下该智能体实际上是如何做到这一点的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Right. Hello everyone. So my name is Rohan and this is a quick demo on the optimization agent that what has been recently built. So a quick intro into what the purpose is and what the goal is of this agent and then I'll get into a brief demo and results that uh from a previous run that we just did. So what the goal is here is to build an agent that helps um find the best VLM serving config for any given model. Uh the purpose is to reduce the time it takes to manually um find the best configs and the VLM serving recipe. And if we can automate this and have it run in the background and um have the agent find the recipes and the optimized parameters faster, then it will save a lot of time. So that's the goal of the agent and a little bit more into how the agent actually does this.
</details>

智能体所采用的策略叫做贝叶斯优化（Bayesian optimization），贝叶斯优化是一种用于在任何给定情况下寻找最理想数值的技术。所以在我们的场景下，就是为了在 VL1 方案中找到最理想的数值。呃，它采用数学步骤，从根本上缩小了搜索空间。因此，通过缩小搜索空间，它大大减少了在运行期间寻找该方案中优化数值所花费的时间。贝叶斯优化是所使用的策略，而 Optuna 是在搜索循环中运用该策略的工具。Optuna 使用贝叶斯优化。它向其输入当前的方案，即初始方案。呃，贝叶斯优化在此基础上运行，经过多次迭代或多次试验，它最终找到了优化后的方案，而 Optuna 基本上在每次试验后获取每个方案，对其进行评估，获得一个分数，运用策略来修改方案、改进它，然后将其重新放回搜索循环中运行，直到试验完成。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So the strategy that the agent uses is called Beijing optimization and Beijian optimization is a technique um that's used to find the most optimal numbers um in any given situation. So in our situation it's to find the optimal numbers in the uh VL1 recipe. uh it uses mathematical steps that essentially minimizes the search space. So by minimizing the search space, it uh reduces a lot of the time that it takes in finding the optimized uh numbers in this uh recipe during the run. So B uh basian optimization is a strategy that is used but optuna is the tool that uses a strategy in the search loop. So, Optuna uses Beijian optimization. It feeds it the current recipe, the starting recipe. Uh, Beijian optimization runs on that and over many iterations or many trials, it eventually finds the optimized recipe and Optina basically takes each recipe after every trial, assesses it, gets a score, um, uses a strategy to change the recipe, improve it, and then it reruns it within the, uh, search loop um, until the trials is done.
</details>

那么我们来稍微深入看一下我们是如何操作的。我现在正在这里的一个 droplet 实例中，例如这个 droplet 加载了 GPT OSS 12B 模型。我们可以做的就是查看当前的方案。我们在这里看到的是我们用来测试它的四种工况（regimes）。有 chat short，有 chat long，有 rag 和 batch classify。呃，它们有各自输入的一组 ISL 和 OSL token。呃，所有这些都可以根据我们想要看到的结果来进行更改。但本质上，这就是你在下载模型权重时会获得的初始配置、初始方案。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So let's go a little bit into how uh we do this. So I'm currently in a droplet here and this droplet for example has GPT OSS uh 12B model loaded up. So what we can do is uh we can look at the current um recipe. So what we see here is four regimes right that we're testing it on. So there's chat short, there's chat long, there's rag and batch classify. uh they have their own set of ISL OSL tokens that we're feeding it through. Uh all these um can be changed depending on what uh we want to see. But essentially this is the starting uh config starting recipe that you would get when you download the weights of the model.
</details>

嗯，接下来一旦你的 droplet 下载好了模型，并且你准备好运行这个智能体，你就可以粘贴进这条命令，呃，它基本上会告诉你权重的来源在哪里。嗯，激活一个虚拟环境并运行名为 tuner.py 的脚本。呃，这个脚本基本上需要你指定几项内容。你需要给它指定研究名称（study name）。你需要告诉它初始的 YAML 文件，也就是我们刚才在上面看到的那个。你需要告诉它运行所在的硬件。你还需要告诉它你想要的试验次数（trials）。呃，在这个示例中我们设定了 24 次。嗯，我简短地展示另一个过去运行过的示例，那次只运行了 9 次试验。所以，嗯，无论如何，这就是运行的命令。呃，通常这些命令需要运行几个小时。我想对于 GPL，上面显示运行了大约四到五个小时，然后对于另一个模型，也花了几个小时。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um so moving once you have the model downloaded in your droplet and you're ready to run this agent you paste in this command uh which essentially tells you you know where the where the weights are coming from. um activate a virtual um environment and have um uh or not have but run the script called tuner.py. Uh this script essentially tells you or it has a couple of things you have to tell it. So you you want to give it the name of the study. You want to tell it the starting uh YAML file which we just saw above here. You want to tell it the hardware that it's running on. And you want to tell it the number of trials that you want. So uh in this example we have 24. um briefly I'll show you another example of a past run that was run uh that had only nine trials. So um uh anyway uh this is the command that's that's run. ### 管理大型 AI 编程项目的熵增

嗨，我是 Dimmitri。在本次分享中，我将展示我是如何开展大型 AI 编程项目的维护工作的。编码智能体让写代码变得廉价，也让熵增变得更加廉价。在漫威电影中，灭霸眼睁睁看着自己的母星泰坦星在自身的无序膨胀下走向崩溃。因此，当宇宙变得拥挤时，他抹除了所有生命的一半。这次演讲讲的是我在一个项目中做出同样举措的那一次经历。我们的代码当时就像泰坦星那样不断膨胀。当我发现智能体干了什么之后，我删掉了没人用得上的测试和样板代码。我不得不重写整个项目，并且丢失了一些有用的组件。这就是两难之境：要么淹没在混乱中，要么破坏掉人们正在使用的功能。在这里，我们将为那个项目构建第三种选择。重写并没有维持太久。智能体一直在写，混乱又重新滋生。所以我在其上方划了一条界线。变动之多，已经超出了任何人的审查能力。第三种选择是保持在其承载能力之下，进行小范围清理

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Hi, I'm Dimmitri. In this session, I will show how I work on maintaining large AI coding projects. Coding agents made code cheap. They made entropy even cheaper. In the Marvel movie, Thanos watched his home planet Titan collapse under its own growth. So, when the universe got crowded, he raised half of everything. This talk is about the one time I did the same in one project. Our code was growing the way Titan did. When I found what my agents had done, I deleted tests and boil plate code nobody used. I had to rewrite the whole project and I lost some useful pieces. And that's the dilemma. Drown in chaos or break what people use. And here we'll build the third option for that project. The rewrite didn't hold. The agents kept writing and the mass grew back. So I drew a line above it. There is more change than anyone can check. The third option is to stay under it with small sweeps
</details>

该系统的大部分是纯文本语言描述，据我估计大约占四分之三。所以文本表达同样需要工程化。刚开始我们借鉴了变异测试：你故意改坏一句话，然后观察测试是否变红。在这里，几乎没有任何测试变红。如果你的包装层全是代码，那么你的智能体指令和测试就是你的自然语言文本。对于智能体来说，重写很容易，难点在于知道重写后必须保留什么。对于代码，测试可以告诉你；而对于文本，只有人能告诉你。智能体无法独立构建复杂的系统。它们只修复容易看到的问题，并在此过程中破坏一致性。而人也会疲劳，所以我们只在必要的地方消耗他们的判断力。当业务方与团队交流时，背后隐藏着一个真正的问题：这是一个需要人工处理的步骤，还是我们可以将其自动化？业务专家无法回答这个问题，他们看不到系统内部是如何连接的。因此，开发人员利用他们的代码编写智能体深入排查追踪链和日志，由他们来决定属于哪一种。在我们的任务中，团队主管的决策仍然保留人工处理。其限制在于我们能够关联的数据。

### 第二章：在何处仍需人类判断

第二章。专家一次修正一个任务，而智能体则整天运行同样的文本逻辑。我们追踪每一个被接受或拒绝的结果。准确率向我们展示了哪些地方仍然需要人工介入。我过去以为目标是将一切都搬到代码中。那么，我们在哪里仍然需要判断力？再次回到我们的任务：文本说明写着上限是 1,500，而代码写的却是一千。同一个订单在一个地方没问题，在另一个地方却超限了，但没有任何东西抛出错误。甚至像“不要调用 X”这样的一行字，也会让模型知道 X 的存在。这就是第三颗宝石——现实。它是真的，还是对模型来说仅仅看起来是真的？自然语言文本没有编译器，所以我们自己构建了一个。然后，作者将每个修改后的文件进行静态检查，就像代码一样记录进账本。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>

I used to think the goal was to move everything into code. So where do we still need judgment? Here is our task again. The pro says the limit is 1,500. The code says a th00and. The same order is fine in one place and too high in the other and nothing throws an error. Even a line like do not call X teaches the model that X exists. That's the third stone reality. Is it true or does it only look true for the model? Pros has no compiler. So we built one. Then the author lints every changed file into ledger just like code.
</details>

静态检查无法阻止多份副本之间发生漂移，所以我们观察执行轨迹。一条轨迹就是一个智能体穿行于文本逻辑中的路径。当智能体不断走在同一条路径上时，我们就会将其转化为一个工作流工具，并删除走过该路径的文本描述。我们最初的工作流工具把事情搞得更糟了：文本里仍然列出了工具内部的步骤，导致智能体把那些步骤又重复执行了一遍。一旦文本中只保留决策，它就奏效了。现在，一条执行轨迹只有在它不断重复且在各个国家之间保持一致时，才会变成代码，否则它就依然保留为文本形式。那些仍然存在差异的部分，作为文本保留在框架之内。代码负责准备事实数据并执行决策。专家拥有决策表的所有权，而模型只负责挑选某一行。如果没有合适的行，就由人工接管。下一步我们还没有构建完成：模型将承担微小的决策，并由工作流将它们串联起来。这个边界是双向移动的。我们将数百行表达式从代码移入文本逻辑中，因为专家看不懂代码；而正则表达式用来识别关键信号依然很好用。当数量变多时，它们就会被移到配置中。在专家能够读懂的前提下，只要答案存在变数，判断力就保留在那里。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Lind can't stop copies drifting aparts. So we watched the walks. A walk is the path one agent takes through the pros. When agents keep walking in the same path, we turn it into one workflow tool and we delete the pros that walked it. Our first workflow of tools made things worse. The pros still named the steps inside them. So agents run those steps again. It worked once. The pros kept only the decisions. Now a walk becomes code only when it repeats and agrees across countries otherwise it stays pros. What still varies stays in pros inside the frame. Code prepares the facts and carries out the decision. Experts own the decision table and the model only picks a row. If no row fits, a person takes over. We haven't built the next step yet. ### 第三章：保持受控的真正含义

第三章。现在由模型做出决定，其决定会转化为行动。我过去以为控制意味着对智能体的权限控制。那么，保持受控到底意味着什么？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Chapter three. Now the model decides and its decision becomes an action. I used to think control meant permissions on the agent. So what does it mean to stay in control?
</details>

控制始于模型能看到什么，这就是第四颗宝石——心灵。模型知道什么？当我们让它自主选择加载什么时，它把所有东西都加载进来了。然后仅仅因为价格信息可见，它就凭空捏造了一个价格校验。任何留在其上下文中的内容都会变成行动的理由。对于我们的任务，由运行时环境决定模型能看到什么：那是来自订单的三个事实数据。完整记录保留在运行时环境中，模型犯错的举动就会减少。以下是我们如何减少大型集合的上下文膨胀：模型传递一个句柄，即运行时环境已经持有的数据的一个简短名称。在发生任何写操作之前，运行时环境会对其进行校验。这既保持了上下文体量小巧，又足以支持做出决策。与此同时，它还会针对一致性对上下文进行校验。一个掌握正确事实的模型仍然可能造成破坏。这就是第五颗宝石——力量。什么引发了修改？我们在开发期间以及在运行时写入时对修改进行双重控制。只有代码能打开写入路径，因此我们每天可以修改文本逻辑很多次。那些开放的环，就是仍然依赖于人的部分。让我们放大来看看执行写入：五道防护墙立在它前面，第六道则检查它是否已落地。如果某次写入看起来失败了，智能体就会重试，日志流水确保它只执行一次。控制的意义远不止于对智能体的权限设置。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Control starts with what the model sees. That's what the fourth stone mind. What does the model know? When we let it choose what to load, it loaded everything. Then it invented a pricing check just because prices were visible. Whatever sits in its context becomes a reason to act. The runtime decides what's the model sees for our task. That's three facts from the order. The full record stays with the runtime and the model makes fewer wrong moves. Here how we minimize context blo for a big set. The model passes a handle, a short name for date that the runtime already holds. The runtime checks it before any write. It keeps the context small and still enough for the decision. At the same time, it uh validates context for consistency. A model with the right facts can still do damage. That's the fifth stone, power. What made change? We control a change twice. during development and again at the right at runtime. Only code opens the right path so we can change pros many times a day. The open rings are the parts that still depend on people. Let's zoom in and run right. Five walls stand in front of it. And the sixth checks that it landed. If a right looks like it failed, an agent retries and the journal makes sure it runs only once. Control means much more than permissions on the agent.
</details>

### 第四章：注意力保护与系统失控的信号

第四章。大多数日子里我都掠过系统表面。从高处看，一切显得井井有条，因此我不会深入到智能体所做的每一处修改中。接着，表面开始变得粗糙。我们如何知道自己正在失去控制？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Chapter four. Most days I glide over the system. From up here, everything looks fine. So I don't go down into every change my agents made. Then the surface gets rough. How do we know we're losing control?
</details>

所有那些控制机制保护了系统，却没有保护我的注意力。一开始，我阅读每一处修改，系统也正是借此吸收了我的习惯。后来，变更的体量增长到了单个人无法全部阅读的程度。我陷入了精疲力竭，不再阅读每一处修改。那就是最后一颗宝石的代价。那么，由谁来评判，代价又是什么？有了它，无限手套才算完整。我认识的每一位技术主管都经历过这个境地：成为所有变更都在等待的那一个人。智能体只是让你在几周内而不是几年内就陷入这个局面。我需要一种不依赖单个人头脑的工作方式。而第一个衡量指标就是我自己：当我感到疲惫且无法解释修改了什么时，那就是信号。在该指标告警的同时，仪表盘可能全都是绿色的。所以我停下来，先修复流程，而不是等待某个数据指标出现。我无法再阅读每一份 diff，因此我构建了用于监控事物变化规律及其导数的仪表工具。其中之一就是爆炸半径。在我们构建一个新的检查项之前，我们曾在数千个历史会话中回放过其规则，它原本触发的概率还不到 1%。在数值发生跳变的地方，我会深入下去仔细阅读。当某些东西看起来有问题时，我们会等待：单个信号会等到它再次出现，即便它来自一位专家也是如此。而在多个分片中出现的集群问题则不同，那时我们会统一修复一次其背后的概念。如果听任不管，智能体会不断添加条件，直到今天的任务全部通过；当你把这些条件移除时评测得分会下降，而你将得到一份真实的不足清单。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
All those controls protect the system. Nothing protected my attention. At the start, I read every change and that's how the system picked up my habits. Then the volume grew past what one person can read. I was burning out and I stopped reading every change. That was the price of the last stone. So, who judges and what does it cost? With it, the gauntlet is complete. Every tech lead I know has been here. the one person every change waits on. Agents just get you there in weeks instead of years. A cluster across slices is different. Then we fix the concept behind it once. Left alone, an agent keeps adding conditions until today's tasks pass. The eval score drops when you take them out and you get an honest list of gaps.

### 内部研发吞吐量与软件工厂实践

Cognition 的首席产品官。我们打造了一款名为 Devon 的 AI 产品。不过今天我大部分时间想从产品本身抽离出来一点，重点讲讲我们在工程团队内部是如何运作以大幅提高速度的。作为这一切的背景，这就是 Cognition 目前合并 PR 的吞吐量情况。大约始于 12 个月前，所以大家看到的大致是一年以来的快照。而在过去这一年里，这里蓝色的图表，这个 40 倍，代表我们工程团队产出的合并 PR 数量增长了 40 倍。现在大家可能也注意到了，底部有一条很细的黑线。它很容易被忽略，但那个 2.7 倍正是我们同期工程人员规模的增长。所以我们一直在招聘工程师，但我们每位工程师现在的生产力是以前的 10 倍以上。而且我敢说，这些 PR 的质量即使没有更高，也绝不亚于一年前。因此，我今天真正想探讨的核心就是：我们究竟做了什么，才释放了这 40 倍的工程吞吐量？从代码库到底层工程团队的组织方式，我们做了很多事情，我认为如果大家想复制同样的结果，这些都是非常实用的落地步骤。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
chief product officer at Cognition. We build a AI product called Devon. I want to spend most of my time here today though kind of abstracting a bit away from that and and focusing on what are we doing internally as an engineering team to move way faster. And so the backdrop I would put for all this is this is what our merged PR throughput looks like at Cognition. Uh the start of this is around 12 months ago. So you're looking at about a snapshot of a year. And over this last year, this blue chart here, this 40x is a 40x in the number of merged PRs we are producing on our engineering team. Now you might also notice there's a small black line along the bottom there. It's easy to miss, but that 2.7x that is our engineering headcount growth in the same period. So we have been hiring engineers, but each of our engineers is now more than 10x more productive than they were before. And I would dare to say that these PRs are just as high quality, if not higher quality, than they were a year ago. And so really the crux of what I want to talk about today is what did we do to unlock this 40x engineering throughput. And there's a lot of things we did from both the code base to the way we structure engineering teams that I think are very practical steps you can take if you want to reproduce the same results.
</details>

大家很多人可能都熟悉软件工厂。你们当中有多少人听说过软件工厂？让我看看有多少人举手。太棒了。那么，我会先从基础讲起，然后再逐步深入到更进阶的内容。软件工厂的基本理念就是无需任何人类参与。不过很多时候仍然需要人类的判断，那时我们就会引入升级上报机制，但默认情况下，我们的整个软件开发流程端到端全由 Devon 运行。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Many of you are probably familiar with the software factory. How many of you guys have heard of software factory? Let's see a raise of hands. Cool. So, I'll start with the basics and then I'll work my way up to the more advanced stuff. The basic idea of a software factory is without a single human involved. Oftent time human judgment is needed though and that's where we have escalations but by default our entire software development process is just run by Devon from end to end.
</details>

这意味着的一件事就是，软件工程师的角色必须发生相当大的改变。大约在今年 3 月，我们与工程团队进行了这样一次对话，我们说：“听着，如果你现在只是从 Linear 任务看板上认领工单，然后发给智能体去实现，那么在 AI 时代你真的很难取得成功，因为任何能够被描述且能够被实现的任务，我们都能交给 Devon。” 所以说，每个人都必须改变自己的研发方式。而我们推行这一转变的方式，就是将原来的大团队拆分成一个个更小的 pod。在这些小 pod 中，工程师们不仅能获得成群的智能体替他们工作，还被赋予了更多的垂直业务职责。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
One of the things this means is that the role of the software engineer has to change quite a bit. Around March of this year, we had this conversation with the engineering team and we said, "Look, if you are right now just picking off tickets off a linear board and then sending that to an agent and implementing it, you're not going to really be set up for success in the age of AI because anything that is described and can be implemented, we'll be able to send to Devon." So really, everyone has to change the way that they're developing. And the way we've enforced this is we've taken our larger teams and we've broken them up into smaller pods. In these smaller pods, not only are given swarms of agents to do work for them, they are given more vertical responsibility.
</details>

因此，一个团队在某一时刻可能只有两到三名工程师，但他们同时还要负责工程开发之外的一切事务。他们要负责倾听客户的 Bug 反馈、进行 QA 测试，还要承担 PM 工作与产品管理职责。基本上，每位工程师都在管理自己一整套待办事项的 Linear 看板，并将任务派发给智能体替他们实现。所以，这确实是我们工程师工作方式上的一次根本性变革。这是我们过去一年中推行的举措之一，同时我们也在帮助许多大型组织自行落地这一模式。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So instead, you might have only two or three engineers on a team at a time, but they're responsible for everything beyond the engineering as well. They're responsible for listening to customer bug reports. They're responsible for doing QA testing. They're responsible for doing the PMing and product management. And basically, every engineer manages their entire own linear board of things to do. and they send that to an agent to implement for them. So, it's really a fundamental change in the way that our engineers do work. This has been one of the things that we've implemented over the last year and we've been helping a lot of large organizations implement for themselves as well.
</details>

我还想重点说明的另一点是，我们全面在 Slack 中开展工作。举例来说，客户反馈的 Bug 报告大致是这样的：他们在 Slack 上给我发来一段视频，我们就直接把 Bug 报告转发到内部频道，@ 标注 Devon 并说：“嘿，请修复这个。” Devon 会立即响应，提交一份 PR，但同时还会附带发回一段带有修复和验证证明的视频。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Another thing I'd really like to highlight is we work from Slack everywhere. So, this is an example of what a customer bug report might look like. They send me a video on Slack and we just forward bug reports straight into our internal channel and we tag Devon and say, "Hey, please fix this." Devon immediately comes up, shows up a PR, but then also sends back a video with the fix and proof.
</details>

这让我们的工作轻松了太多，因为在大多数工作中我完全不必离开 Slack。我们把所有的开发工作都抽象成了基于证据和视频，我可以直接把修复就绪的证明发回给客户，多数时候甚至连 IDE 或 GitHub 都不需要打开。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And this makes our lives so much easier because I literally don't have to leave Slack for most of my work. We've abstracted away all the development to be based on evidence, videos, and I can just send the proof that the fix is ready right back to the customer without having open IDE or even GitHub most of the time.
</details>

所以，这就是大家最终希望达到的目标状态：即你可以在不打开 IDE、不打开任何一个编码智能体界面的情况下，完成整个开发生命周期。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So, this is kind of the end goal you want to reach, which is a point where you can do your entire development life cycle without opening up an IDE or without opening up a single coding agent.
</details>

### 底层基础设施改造与本地沙箱化

但要达到这种状态，我们必须在底层做出大量的基础设施改造。对于任何想要实现这一目标的工程团队而言，这确实都是最硬核、最困难的部分。这绝不是单靠采购一个编码智能体就能解决的，你必须去改变自己的构建方式。以前在测试应用程序时，我们会针对云端部署进行测试。我们有预发环境，会连接到预发数据库，连接到线上活跃服务器，然后让工程师手动点击去端到端验证流程是否正常。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
But to get to this point, there's a lot of infrastructure we had to change underneath. And so this is the hard part that really every engineering team has to do if they want to get to that point. It's not just you purchase a coding agent. You got to go change the way you build. So before when we test our applications, we'd be testing them against cloud deployments. We'd have a staging environment, connect to a staging database, connect to live servers, and then we'd have our engineers manually go click through and verify that the flows work end to end.
</details>

我们进行了一次大规模迁移，使得每个服务都能通过 Docker Compose 在本地运行。针对每一个第三方服务，我们都制作了可以本地运行的 Mock 版本。这些全都可以在单个虚拟机内运行，并且每个 Devon 都能访问这些虚拟机。现在，我们公司内部的每一个编码智能体在测试代码变更时，完全不需要触及任何线上活跃服务。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
We did a big migration to move every service to be able to run locally with Docker Compose. We've made it so every third party service we have mocked versions to be able to run locally. And these can all run inside a single virtual machine that every Devon has access to. And every coding agent in our company now does not need to touch any live services at all to test their changes.
</details>

事实上，在公司内部我们每小时都会重建这些虚拟机。每隔一小时，我们就会拉取最新版本的代码。我们会从零开始全量重建整个代码库，以便能从零快速、轻松地运行起来。并且，如果由于代码库迭代过快导致这些构建失败，我们同样会让 Devon 作为第一响应者。它们总能立即着手修复这些虚拟机快照。因此，每当我们从 Slack 启动一个智能体时，它访问的都是一台全新的机器，运行着带有最新更新的全新版本代码库。顺便提一句，对于这些智能体，你也必须教会它们如何去测试你的代码库。不过使用云端编码智能体的好处在于，它们能够自我维护其训练闭环。在刚才提到的测试部分中，Devon 会自己编写关于如何测试我们代码库的技能。代码库会随时间演进，测试它的方式也会发生改变，但 Devon 能够不断更新这些技能，从而一次又一次地对其进行测试。这样带来的最终结果就是，大家真的再也不需要打开笔记本电脑去测试任何代码变更了。事实上，Cognition 的好几位工程师已经完全转向使用 iPad 来进行日常开发流程了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
They in fact these VMs, we rebuild them every hour at the company. Every hour we pull the latest version of the code. We rebuild the entire codebase from scratch to be able to run very quickly and easily from scratch. Um, and if these builds ever fail because your codebase is rapidly changing, uh, we also have Devon be the first response there. And they're always immediately fixing these VM snapshots. So every time we spin up an agent from Slack, it goes to a fresh machine on a fresh version of the codebase with the latest updates. Um, and by the way, these agents, you need to teach them how to test your codebase as well. But the benefit of using cloud coding agents is that they can self-maintain their training loops. So that last section there on testing, Devon will write its own skills for how to test our codebase. The codebase will change over time. So the way you test it will change, but Devon can keep on updating these skills so that tests it over and over again. So the end result of this is literally people do not need to open their laptops anymore to test any code changes. In fact, several engineers at Cognition have already moved to using iPads for their development process.
</details>

顺便说一下，iPad 也更安全。大家知道，最近看到了很多安全入侵事件，其中很大一部分是因为 DevOps 以及人员泄露秘钥造成的。谁知道开发者的机器上都装了些什么？你的电脑里可能到处都散落着各类敏感凭证。而当一切都置于云端这些受控的虚拟机中时，你的安全状况实际上要好得多。

By the way, iPads are also more secure. You know, a lot you've seen a lot of these hacks recently. A lot of it is because of DevOps and and people leaking keys. 我已经展示过一些可供测试的基础场景。我们还测试过高级的多人协作配置，这非常酷。以往在开发 Devon 接入 Zoom 通话进行语音交流的功能时，我们实际上会让测试中的 Devon 自行创建多个账号，让它们全部从同一台虚拟机内加入同一个 Zoom 会议，并来回发送语音备忘录，以确保功能正常运行。因此，如果你真正挖掘代码库的极限，就能实现相当高级的配置方案。

We've oftent times when we were developing Devon's ability to join voice in Zoom calls, we actually had Devon when it tests create multiple accounts, have them join the same Zoom call all from within their one VM and send voice memos back and forth to make sure that the functionality is working. So you can do quite advanced setups if you really push the limits of your codebase.

### 递归自我优化机制

而在这一切之上，大家显然都想要实现递归式的自我优化。我认为大家往往大多是从模型层面来考虑这一点的，但实际上大家细想一下，如果配置了云端智能体，你整个代码库中编写的每一行代码、生成的每一个 PR、做出的每一项决策，全都会被完整追踪记录下来。既然如此，完全没有理由不在此基础之上也运行智能体，去执行数据分析，并找出我们究竟在哪里浪费了时间、在哪里浪费了 Token、哪些环节可以做成脚本，以及我们能够添加哪些测试来改进这一流程。因此我们设立了一个专属频道，Devon 每天都会在里面发布想法，比如：“嘿，如果我们更改 Node 配置，大概能帮 CI 节省大约一分钟时间。如果我们修改这个 Lint 脚本，在这些 Bug 进入 AI 代码审查之前，我们大概能多拦截 20%。” 这确实带来了巨大的突破，你只需要搭建好这些系统，智能体就会主动去为你优化整个软件工厂。这直是

Um, and actually on top of all this, you know, you obviously want recursive self-improvement. And so we have a channel where Devon every day posts ideas about, hey, if we change our node setup, we can say shave like a minute off of our CI. If we change this lint script, we can like catch 20% more of these bugs before they ever hit AI code review. ### 从派发任务到交付职责

我经常听不到大家讨论、但我认为确实是下一步发展方向的事情之一，就是从给智能体分配任务的世界，转向给智能体交付职责的世界。这可以说是一种范式转变。你真的必须开始把这些东西当作同事来对待。那么，给智能体交付职责看起来是什么样的？假设我关心后端性能。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>One of the things that I don't hear people talking about a lot, but I think is really where things are going next is moving from the world where you're giving tasks to your agents to giving jobs to your agents. And this is a bit of a paradigm shift. You really have to start treating these things like co-workers. So what is giving a job to an agent look like? Imagine I care about back-end performance.</details>

实际上，该特性的开发流程应该是这样的。你应该发送给你的智能体：“嘿，我想优化我们应用程序的 P99 延迟。”它不该是由你告诉它该怎么做来实现这点，而是应该说：“好的，P99 延迟，让我来添加遥测指标，弄清楚我们今天的 P99 延迟是多少。让我设置警报，以便我能弄清楚我们未来需要达到什么水平。让我去拆解我们为了能够改变这个延迟所需要做的每一个具体任务。让我创建 Datadog 警报。让我每周在 Slack 中生成报告。并且让我每天在你的手机上给你发送更新，汇报 P99 延迟目前是多少，以及改善了多少。”

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Really, this is what the development flow of the feature should look like. You should send to your agent, hey, I want to improve P99 latency of my application. Instead of you telling it what to do to do that, it should say, okay, P99 latency, let me add the telemetry to figure out where our P99 latency is today. Let me set up alerts so I can figure out where we need to be in the future. Let me go break down every single task we need to do to be able to move this latency. Let me create data dog alerts. Let me create weekly reports in Slack. and let me send you updates on your phone every day on here's where P99 latency is and here's how much it's improved.</details>

从根本上说，这就是我们在 Cognition 鼓励所有工程师构建的系统类型。大多数时候我们甚至都不希望你去提示你的智能体。我们希望你只给你的智能体设定它们可以在较长时间跨度内为之努力的目标。而且你公司里任何你认为不可衡量的事情，想办法去衡量它。比如，你关心应用程序的性能吗？去测量一下你在应用程序上的点击延迟。如果你的应用程序有很多奇怪的布局偏移，去测量外接矩形框，并尝试找出如何尽量减少整个应用程序中的布局偏移。显然有 P99 延迟，但也有类似入职留存率这样的事情。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>This is fundamentally the type of systems that we encourage all of our engineers to be building a cognition. We don't even we don't even want you to be prompting your agents most of the time. We want you to just be giving your agent goals that they can work toward over long durations of time. And anything in your company that you think isn't measurable, find a way to measure it. You know, do you care about your application performance? Go measure, you know, the latency of clicks on your application. If your application has a lot of weird layout shifts, go measure the bounding boxes and try to figure out how you can minimize layout shifts across your application. There's obviously P9 latency, but there's also things like, you know, onboarding retention.</details>

你们作为工程师所做的工作中，有多少是可以归纳为一个单一数字或指标的？我鼓励你们，如果你能做到这一点，你就应该直接设置一个智能体去 7×24 小时全天候优化那个数字。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>How much of what you guys do as engineers is things you can boil down to a single number or metric. And I encourage you that if you can do that, you should just set up an agent to go optimize that number 247.</details>

### 案例：GPU 推理延迟与安卓应用开发

关于这一点的一个极佳例子就是推理延迟。在 Cognition，我们很幸运成为英伟达新一代 Vera Rubin GPU 的首批接收者。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So a great example of this is inference latency. At Cognition, we're lucky to be the first receivers of Nvidia's new class of Vera Rubin GPUs.</details>

在我们收到这些 GPU 时，内核还没有为它们构建好。因此我们必须从零开始摸索如何在这些即将推出的新硬件之上构建我们自己的推理栈。智能体让我们能够以比以往高得多的效率来完成这项工作。所以我们只是告诉它：“嘿，我们关心这些 GPU 的吞吐量或 TPM。去弄清楚如何将其推向极限。”然后在大约……我想这里大概是一周的时间里，智能体能够去——我们给它分配了一组固定的 GPU 供其开展工作。它能够去寻找不同的策略并测试它们。对于它发现效果最好的策略，它会发送给人类工程师进行最终批准。但基本上大约有八九种不同的事情是通过直接给这些智能体这些高层目标而被大致发现的，我们得以将这些新的 Rubin GPU 的吞吐量从比新的 GB300 更差的状态，提升到如今明显优于上一代 GB300 GPU 的水平。同样，这一切都只是通过给你的智能体一个最终目标来完成的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Kernels were not built for these GPUs or we received them. So we had to from scratch figure out how do we build our own inference stack on top of these this new hardware that's coming out. And agents have allowed us to do this in a way that is much more efficient than before. So we just tell it, hey, we care about the throughput or TPM of these GPUs. Go figure out how to push that to its limits. And over the course of what is about, I guess here like a week, um the agent was able to go, we we allocated it a permanent set of GPUs to work on. It was able to go find different strategies, test them. Uh the strategies that it found worked best, it would send to a human engineer for final approval. But basically there's eight or nine different things that were roughly discovered via just giving these agents these high level goals and we were able to push uh the throughput on these new verbin GPUs from what was worse than the new GB300s to what is now significantly better than the previous class of GB300 GPUs. Um and again this is all done by just giving a end goal to your agents.</details>

另一个我喜欢和大家分享的例子，是我们的工程团队规模相当小。这意味着我们往往没有足够的带宽来为我们可能想做的事情配备充足的人手，比如安卓应用程序。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Another example that I like to uh share with folks, we we are a fairly small engineering team. It means we do not have oftentimes the bandwidth to fully staff things that we might want to do like an Android application.</details>

但是前几天，我们团队的一位工程师 Sam，他有了这个绝妙的想法，他说：“好的，看，我们有一个团队的人正在致力于构建一个出色的 iOS 应用程序。我们难道不能直接把那个当作我们安卓应用程序的蓝图，然后告诉 Devin：‘嘿，去端到端地维护我们从 iOS 应用程序到安卓应用程序的对齐’吗？”而这正是他所做的。但这不仅仅是说，嘿，开一次 Devin 会话去搞定这一切。他说的是：设置常驻的钩子对接我们的代码库以监控 iOS 应用程序；去监听 Slack 频道中用户对我们应用程序的反馈，然后递归地改进我们的应用程序；去对每一个细节进行实时的 QA 测试并确保一切都能运行。实时同步这些东西真是太酷了。我认为最终的结果实际上相当棒。

### 智能体与网络搜索

好的。大家好。我叫 Jeff Pinner。我是 Exa 的 CTO。感谢各位邀请我们来到这里。我们今天打算稍微聊一聊你的智能体所不知道的事情。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>All right. Hello everyone. My name is Jeff Pinner. I'm the CTO of Exa. Thank you guys for having us here. Uh, and we're going to talk a little bit today about things your agent doesn't know.</details>

网络搜索到现在已经存在了大约 30 年，时间长到在现阶段我们对此已经习以为常，而且我们并不会真正去质疑现状，或者质疑它在智能体时代可以有何不同。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So, web search has been around now for about 30 years, long enough where at this point we're kind of used to it and we don't really question the status quo or how it could be different with agents.</details>

在过去大约 18 个月里，搜索迎来了智能体作为一种新的客户群体。它们在代表我们进行搜索。因此，在 Exa，我们专门将网络搜索构建为面向智能体的知识层。我们的请求量已经从两年前的每月约 1250 万次，增长到了刚刚过去的 10 月份的略高于 13 亿次。所以在两年间增长了约 100 倍，而且这并不是 Exa 独有的现象。如果你去交流，OpenAI 刚刚宣布 ChatGPT 处理的搜索量大约是谷歌搜索量的五分之一，而且智能体总体上现在占到了网络流量的大约一半。因此，我认为一个很自然的问题是，为什么智能体到底需要搜索？所以在我们讨论我们如何为智能体做搜索之前，让我们先尝试回答这个问题。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Over the last 18 months or so, search has picked up agents as sort of a new customer. They're searching on our behalf. And so, at EXO, we specifically build web search as a knowledge layer for agents. We've gone from about 12 and a half million requests a month two years ago to a little over 1.3 billion this past October. So about 100x over two years and it's not unique to EXA. If you talk OpenAI just announced that ChatGBT handles about a fifth of Google search volumes and agents in general make up just around half the volume of web traffic now. And so I think the natural question is why do agents need search at all? So let's start with trying to answer this before we talk about how we do search for agents.</details>

前沿模型可以依靠参数化知识完成很多任务。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So frontier models can do a lot of tasks from parametric knowledge.</details>

但遗憾的是，对于现实世界的任务来说，这根本不够。通常前沿模型无论功能多么强大，都总是过时的。模型发布从训练截止到正式推出需要两到五个月的时间。然后在此之后通常还要经过几个月，下一个前沿模型才会发布。因此综合来看，无论最新的知识库是什么，你都落后大约四到八个月。与此同时，世界并不会仅仅因为一个模型从训练阶段转入推理阶段就停止变化。代码库发布、API 变更、价格变动、新法规出台、法院判决产生。世界在继续前行。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>But unfortunately for real world real world tasks that simply isn't enough. Generally frontier models no matter how capable they are are always out of date. Model launches take two to five months from training cuto off to launch. Then there's generally a few months after that before the next frontier model is released. And so taking that together, you're about four to eight months out of date with whatever the latest knowledge base is. And in the meantime, the world doesn't stop changing just because a model has moved from training to inference. Libraries launch, APIs change, prices change, new regulations appear, court rulings happen. Um, the world continues to go.</details>

而且即使事实并非如此，训练数据本身实际上也只是模型所接受训练知识的一小部分。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And even if that weren't true, training data itself is actually just a small amount of knowledge that the model is trained on.</details>

目前最大规模的模型是在大约 50 万亿个 token 上训练的。网络本身大约有 3100 万亿个 token。这还包括诸如登录系统和应用程序背后的深层网络。如果你将其缩小到仅被建立索引的网络，也就是搜索引擎能够看到的部分，那仍然大约有 510 万亿个 token。这意味着即使在模型截止发生后世界没有继续演变，你仍然……

<details><summary>Original English</summary>The biggest models out there are trained on around 50 trillion tokens. The web itself is around 3,100 trillion. And that includes the deep web behind things like login and apps. If you focus it down to just the web that is indexed, the thing that search engines can see, that's still about 510 trillion tokens. Which means even if the world didn't continue to evolve after the model cut off happened, you would still</details>

<!-- chunk 18/52 -->

……只索引了模型必须去寻找的外界知识中极小的一部分。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>only be indexing a very small amount of the knowledge that's out there that models have to go and find.</details>

而对于我们希望模型去做的任务类型，希望智能体去做的任务类型，有许多基于外部知识的有价值工作正在开展。无论是在编程、法律工作，还是尽职调查工作中，通常所有这些任务都需要跨多个来源获取正确且完整的知识。因此，这正是我们试图弥合的差距。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And for the type of tasks we want models to do, we want agents to do, there is valuable work that happens that is based on outside knowledge. Whether it's across coding, legal work, due diligence work, generally all these tasks require correct complete knowledge across multiple sources. And so this is what we're trying to the gap we're trying to close.</details>

### Atlas 基准与模型检索表现

现在，在尝试弥合任何差距时，首先要做的事情就是尝试对其进行衡量。因此，我们做的第一件事就是创建了一个名为 Atlas 的基准，用来测试评测架构（harnesses）利用现实世界知识的能力。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Now, the first thing to do when we're trying to close any gap is to try to measure it. And so, the first thing we do is create a benchmark called Atlas, which tries to test how well harnesses can leverage real world knowledge.</details>

所以，我们创建了 Atlas 基准来尝试在这些任务中测试智能体。

首先，我们要求智能体找到符合条件的实体。

然后，我们要求智能体利用它们所能找到的尽可能多的信息来丰富这些知识。

最后，我们对照标准答案（golden answer key）给它们评分。

我们只将正确识别出我们寻找的所有维度的行计为正确。因此，要在 Atlas 上取得成功，你需要始终保持正确，而不仅仅是对单次搜索拥有不错的命中率。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So, we created the Atlas benchmark to try to test agents in these tasks.

First, we require agents to find entities that match a condition.

Then, we require agents to enrich the knowledge with as much information as they can find.

And finally, we score them against the golden answer key.

And we only count rows correct that correctly identify all aspects of what we're looking for. And so to succeed at Atlas, you need to be consistently right and not just have a decent hit rate for individual searches.</details>

因此，我们将以此作为衡量智能体在知识检索方面表现的基准。我们发现的第一件事是，模型记忆会随着时间推移（新近度）而衰退。

我们在 Atlas 上测试智能体时看到，模型成功率一旦遇到训练集未覆盖的数据，就会急剧下降。事实越新，模型知道的就越少。而模型需要——或者我应该说智能体需要——数据来尝试完成这些任务。因此，我们首先能看到的是，仅靠记忆、仅靠参数化记忆（parametric memory）是不够的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So we're going to use this as a benchmark for how we measure agent performance when it comes to knowledge retrieval. And the first thing that we find is that model memory falls off with recency.

When we test agents on Atlas, we see that model success rates fall off sharply as soon as they enter as soon as they encounter data that's not covered in the training set. The newer the fact is, the less the models know. And models require or I should say agents require data to try to achieve these tasks. And so the first thing that we can see is that memory alone, parametric memory isn't sufficient.</details>

因此，当我们运行仅依赖参数化记忆而没有网络搜索的智能体时，它们在 Atlas 上的得分在 9% 左右。所以我们将以此来尝试定量理解网络搜索是如何提升性能的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So when we run agents with just parametric memory without web search, they score around 9% on Atlas. And so we'll use this to try to understand quantitatively how web search improves performance.</details>

### 智能体搜索的三大挑战

那么接下来的一个自然而然的问题是：为什么它们不直接去 Google 搜一下呢？[哧笑]

第一个原因是，智能体搜索的方式与人类不同。

通常人们在 Google 中输入大约 3 个词。呃，这比 6 年前的大约 2 个词有所增加。另一方面，智能体搜索时使用的或多或少是完整的句子。在 Exa 上的平均搜索查询大约是 12 个词。Exa 上大约 10% 的查询长达 17 个词或更多。

而这些词增加了限制条件、版本信息、日期、地点，以及各种不同类型的条件，这些都是基于关键字排名的系统原本并非为了处理这些条件而设计的。因此我们来看一个例子。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So the next natural question is why don't they just Google it? [snorts]

And the first reason is agents don't search like people.

Generally people type about three words into Google. Uh, and that's up from about two words six years ago. Agents, on the other hand, search with more or less full sentences. The mean search on X is about 12 words. About 10% of queries on EXA are 17 words or higher.

And these words add constraints, versioning information, dates, places, all different types of conditions on the on the search that keyword ranking wasn't really designed to handle. And so we'll take a look at an example of that.</details>

这是来自 Exa 的一个真实查询。它是一条寻找公司的请求。它是一个完整的句子，添加了一堆限制条件。当你把它输入到关键字检索算法中时，你真正得到的是寥寥几个根本没有回答查询所问内容的结果。呃，搜索并没有理解这个查询。其中第三个结果明确位于美国境内，而查询所要求的却是美国境外的数据。

几分钟后我们还会回过头来看这个。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So this is an actual query from Exa. It's a request for companies. It's a full sentence. It adds a bunch of constraints. And when you feed this into a keyword retrieval algorithm, what you really get is a handful of results that don't really answer what the query asked for. Uh the search didn't understand the query. The result, the third one is specifically in the US and the result asks for data outside the US.

And so we're going to come back to this in a few minutes.</details>

因为我想聚焦的第二件事是这样一个事实：对于网络上的许多知识，搜索根本无法触及。大多数有价值的信息并不在公开网络上。报道的新闻、人员数据、财务数据、研究，它们都处于付费墙之后。因此，抓取开放互联网的搜索引擎永远看不到这些页面。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Because the second thing I want to focus on is the fact that for a lot of knowledge on the web, search just doesn't have access to it. Most of the valuable information isn't on the open web. Reported news, people data, financial data, research, they're all paywalled off. And so search engines that crawl the open internet don't ever see these fa pages.</details>

最后，即使知识位于公开网络上且你的智能体可以访问它，还存在第三个问题，那就是效率。

你知道，如果一个智能体进行 10 次搜索，从该搜索中读取 5 个页面，从每个页面获取大约 16k 的原始 HTML，仅在处理结果上就大约需要 860,000 个 token。如果你还记得第一张幻灯片，我们每月大约处理 13 亿次请求。因此，仅仅为了提取信息就有大量的 token 在被消耗翻转。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And then lastly, even when knowledge is on the open web and your agents can access it, there's a third problem which is efficiency.

You know, if an agent makes 10 searches, reads five pages from that search, gets about 16k of raw HTML from each page, that's about 860,000 tokens just in processing results. And if you remember from the first slide, we serve about 1.3 billion requests a month. So that's a lot of tokens that are just churning to extract information.</details>

最后，当你加入关键字搜索并查看我们 Atlas 基准的情况时，得分确实有所提高。它补充了最新的知识，但我们只上升到了大约 28%。因为大多数行返回的数据依然是不完整的，或者干脆就是错误的值。

因此，当我们审视搜索时，面临的挑战是如何真正向智能体提供它们实际需要的知识。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And then finally, when you add keyword search and we look at what happens to our Atlas benchmark, the score definitely improves. It's added recent knowledge, but we've gone up to about 28%. Because most of the rows still come back with incomplete data or just wrong values.

And so the challenge when we look at search is how do we actually provide the knowledge to agents that they actually require.</details>

### Exa 的语义检索与知识扩展解决方案

所以，我刚才提到我们会回到这个查询，至少在 Exa，我们尝试解决这项任务所做的第一件事就是从信息中提取含义，而不是仅做句法匹配或关键字匹配。

因此我们通读页面，理解查询的意图，在这里我们向你提供明确回答该意图并附带所有条件的结果。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So the first thing I told you we'd come back to this query is at least at EXA the first thing we do to try to tackle this task is to extract meaning out of information as opposed to just syntactical matches or keyword matches.

And so we look through pages, we understand the intent of queries, and here we give you results that specifically answer the intent with all of the conditionals attached.</details>

为了做到这一点，我们采取了针对现代网络爬虫的做法。我们爬取互联网，进行语义提取，将其嵌入到向量空间中，并建立索引。当你的查询进入时，我们将查询嵌入到同一个向量空间中，并找到最近的匹配项。这使我们能够从句法理解和关键字匹配转向对查询和网络内容的语义理解。

我们在高达每秒一百万页的处理量下执行此操作，呃，从抓取到建立索引的延迟大约为三分钟。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And so in order to do this, we do what you would for a modern web crawler. We crawl the internet. We do a semantic extraction. We embed it in vector space. We index it. When your query comes in, we embed the query in the same vector space. and we find the nearest matches. And this lets us move from syntactical understanding and keyword matching to a semantic understanding of the query in the web.

And we do this at a volume of up to a million pages a second uh with a latency of about three minutes from crawl to index.</details>

现在来看第二个问题，我们需要将其扩展到公开网络之外。

因此在这里，我们必须通过合作的专有数据以及我们提取和表示的知识图谱数据，来扩展公共网络上的内容。这样，智能体就可以检索到它针对自身问题所需的提问与答案，而无需知道去哪里寻找它们。这一切都在单个 API 之后完成。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Now for the second problem, we need to extend it just beyond the open web.

And so here we have to extend what's on the public web with both proprietary data that's partnered and knowledge graph data that we extract and represent. And this way the agent can retrieve questions and answers that it needs to its questions without having to know where to look for them. Doing this behind a single API.</details>

针对最后一个挑战，为了支撑智能体产生的查询量，我们不能简单地将它们引向网页。我们必须从页面中提取答案，并为智能体创建高 token 密度的表示形式以供处理。仅仅说明该查询的答案存在于这个网页上是不够的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And for the last challenge, in order to support the volume of queries that agents make, we can't simply point them to web pages. We have to extract the answers from the page and create a token dense representation for agents to process. It's not sufficient to say the answer to this query lives on this web page.</details>

你可以拿谁因锂离子电池获得诺贝尔奖这个问题为例。如果只是指向维基百科页面，你将得到数千个字符。

实际对引用和溯源有价值的摘录大约只有 500 个字符。因此，当你必须直接处理来自 Google 的数据时，相比能够直接提取该数据的情况，涉及的 token 大约多出 16 倍。而且这些提取必须具有丰富的语义。你必须挑选出一段能够回答该问题的文本。因此，查询本身至关重要。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>You could take for example this question of who won the Nobel Prize for lithium-ion batteries. If you just point to the Wikipedia page, you'll get thousands of characters.

The actual excerpt that matters for citation and grounding is about 500. So there's about 16x more tokens when you have to process data directly from Google than when you can extract that data. And those extractions have to be semantically rich. You have to pick a segment of the text that answers the question. And so the query matters.</details>

那么这究竟能给我们带来什么呢？

当我们再次使用 Atlas 进行衡量以确证自身时，我们看到我们已经在闭卷测试中从大约 9% 提升到了在整个互联网和扩展知识上进行语义提取时的大约 56%。

因此，我们不仅获得了大约 5 倍的分数提升，抱歉，而且由于提取带来的 token 效率，我们还降低了大约 28% 的成本。由此我们开始定量地证明，改善知识获取确实能提升底层智能体的性能。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And so what does this actually get us?

When we measure with Atlas again to ground ourselves, we've seen that we've improved from about 9% on our closedbook test to about 56% when we do a semantic extraction over the internet and extended knowledge.

So not only do we get about 5x higher of a score, excuse me, but we also reduce the cost by about 28% due to the token efficiency of the extraction. And so we start to demonstrate quantitatively that improving access to knowledge actually improves performance of the underlying agent.</details>

所以，呃，是的，总的来说，我认为我们希望能表达的主要结论是：虽然前沿模型的推理能力相对固定，但通过扩展外围架构并赋予其跨互联网访问语义的能力，可以在智能体性能方面取得很大提升。就这些。谢谢大家。

[掌声]

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And so uh yeah, in general I think the main takeaway that we've hopefully expressed is that while reasoning capabilities on our frontier models are relatively fixed, a lot of improvement can be done in aentic performance by extending the harness and giving it access to semantic meaning across the internet. That's it. Thank you.

[applause]</details>

### Ultra 模式与多智能体编排

那么针对这一点，我们做了什么呢？我们推出了一个叫做 Ultra 模式（ultra mode）的东西。嗯，先说明一下，Ultra 模式目前……如果你去试用并寻找它的话，它目前仍处于开发者预览阶段，所以它位于功能开关（feature flag）之后。但由于它是开源的，代码都在那里。如果你想去试试，你可以体验一下。呃，就像我开头所说的，如果我们自己不喜欢它，我们就不会发布它。所以我们目前仍在对它进行最后的完善。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And so what did we do for that? Well, we launched a thing called ultra mode. Um, ultra mode is still, just to be clear, if you go try it and you look for it, it's still in a developer uh, preview, so it's behind a feature flag, but because it's open source, the code is all there. If you want to go try it, you can you can try it out. Uh, as I said up front, if we don't love it, we don't ship it. So, we're still sort of finishing putting the finishing touches on this one.
</details>

不过我还是想讲一讲它，因为它改变了你的角色——如果我们回顾刚才这一块中间部分，嗯，由我们来充当推动整个工作的人，那些可能就是我们之前在做的事情。其中最大的一项就是盯住问题，然后在问题出现时试图去修复装配线。呃，也许你在评判一个结果，或者你在决定下一步该做什么。Ultra 模式的作用就是替你承担这项工作，对吧？也就是你在你的各个智能体之上再放一个智能体，由它来负责这些事：根据你要达成的任何目标来定义目标、启动任务、监视问题、解决卡点。而这在今天让我们达到了 50 多个会话。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
But I wanted to talk about it anyway because it takes you from being what we did was if you look at this uh, middle part here, um, where we are the person driving the effort, these are the things that we might have been doing. So watching for problems was one of the biggest ones and then trying to fix the assembly line when the problem came up. Uh maybe you're judging a result or you're deciding what to do next. What Ultra Mode does is it does that job for you, right? So you put an agent on top of your agents that then is responsible for doing that, defining the goal based on whatever you're trying to accomplish, starting things, watching for problems, unsticking things. And this got this is getting us today to 50 plus sessions.
</details>

所以如果回想一下我们当初是从单个人和一个会话开始的，现在如果单个开发者就能跑 50 多个会话，这就是为什么能在之前给你们看的 PR 图表中获得那样的体量。而且我们已经能看到即将到来的下一堵墙了。嗯，编排器与其他编排器协同工作，随后围绕你希望它成为什么样的治理机制以及护栏，就变得愈发重要了。对吧？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And so if you think about where we started with a single session and a single person, now if a single developer is doing 50 plus sessions, that's how you get that sort of the volume in the PR chart I showed you earlier. And we can already see the next wall that's coming. Um the orchestrators working with other orchestrators and then the governance around what you want that to be and the guard rails become even more important. Right?
</details>

所以再次说明，这就是我们获得那种体量的方式。那是我们经历过的五个阶段。嗯，对我们来说 Ultra 模式彻底改变了局面，装配线也是一样。呃，如果大家觉得这些想法对自己有用，我鼓励你们去尝试一下。不过我接下来想谈的是，我们从中……我们从中领悟到了什么？究竟是什么在最大程度上阻碍了我们快速交付？

So again, that's how we got the volume. Those were the five phases we went through. The next thing I wanted to talk about though was how do we what did we learn from this? Like what are the biggest things that prevented us from shipping quickly?

### 交付经验：全面测试与审查机制

第一条是测试一切。这并不是……这并不是什么新教训，对吧？软件行业的每个人都明白这一点。我们本就应该这么做。嗯，但我依然太频繁地看到人们……嗯，低估了这所带来的价值。而这一点将与接下来的另一项关于审查的内容结合在一起。比方说，如果去想一想，你发布某样东西的信心究竟来自哪里？我认为很多人依然觉得：“嘿，我之所以有信心，是因为我看过了所有的代码，我确切知道那段代码在做什么。”再说一次，如果你那样做，也许那是一件好事。但是我们获得信心的地方——亚马逊一直以来都是高度测试驱动的，嗯——是来自测试，因为你也许无法每次都去逐一查看代码，但你肯定能够每次都以完全相同的方式去测试某样东西，并检验其结果，对吧？从而得出结果。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The first one is test everything. And now this is no this is not a new lesson, right? Everybody in software knows this. We should be doing this. Um, but I still see people too often um, discount the value this is providing. And this is going to build in with the next one behind this around reviews. Like if you think about where do you get confidence to ship something? I think many people still feel like, hey, I get confidence because I looked at all of the code and I know exactly what that code does. Again, if you do that, like maybe that's a good thing. But where we have gotten our confidence, and Amazon's always been very test driven, um, is from the tests because you might not be able to look at something every time, but you can certainly test something every time in the exact same way and test the result, right? And come out with the result.
</details>

所以对我们来说，质量归根结底变成了一个测试问题。我们目前把相当大一部分时间——大约 20%——专门花在测试上。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And so for us again, quality really becomes a testing problem. And we're spending a significant portion of our time around 20% just on testing today.
</details>

我们学到的另一件大事——也是一件更大的事，同时也是一个有争议的话题——就是审查。那么，大家快速举个手。有多少人曾用智能体做过审查，本以为只会有一两条评审意见，结果回过头一看却心想：“天呐，这代码写得太烂了，对吧？到处都是评审意见。”我们也遇到过同样的问题。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The other big thing we learned, and this is a bigger one and also a contentious topic, is is reviews. So, quick show of hands. How many people have done a review with an agent thinking that there's going to be like one or two comments and then you come back and you're like, "Oh my god, like this is terrible code, right? There's comments everywhere." We had the same problem.
</details>

所以我们最初写了一个审查智能体，它会审查所有的内容，而我们的目标是修改每一条评审意见。好吧，大家……我听到笑声了。如你们所料，你提交了修改。你提交了一个 PR，收到了关于该 PR 的反馈意见。修复之后，你再次提交。回头一看，现在又冒出了三个之前测都没……之前甚至都没提过的新问题，并且在已经修复的地方又冒出了四个问题。你再次提交，这个循环就这么不断重复。因此，我们现在在审查方面所做的，是对审查红线（red reviews）进行分类或分流，分为阻塞性问题和非阻塞性问题。比如破坏构建的情况，嗯，如果有明显导致构建失败的问题，那就是阻塞性问题，在合并这个 PR 之前我们必须先去解决它。改个变量名，没什么大不了的，我们稍后再处理。所以对于这类问题我们就放行通过了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So, we originally wrote a review agent that would review everything and our goal was to fix every single comment. Well, as you guys I heard the laughter. So, as you expect, you you submit changes. So, you submit a PR, you get the feedback on that PR. You come back to you submit it again after you fixed it. You come back and now it's three new things that weren't even test weren't even told about before and it's four things on the things you already fixed. You submit it again and this cycle just continues to repeat. So, what we've done with reviews is now we categorize the red reviews or triage them into blocking and non-blocking issues. So, for example, something that breaks the build, um, if there's something obviously that breaks the build, that's a blocking thing and we need to go fix that before we merge this PR. Changing a variable name, not a big deal. We'll take care of that later. So, we let those things slide through.
</details>

我们在审查方面做的另一件事是，我们[清嗓子]希望智能体能够获得快速的反馈。正如大家在这里可以看到的过程——我稍后会带大家了解——这并不是那么快。呃，所以我们设有一个快速关卡，它会非常迅速地进行复核，确保比如在进入正式审查之前文件都放对了目录。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The other thing that we've done with reviews is we [clears throat] want to have fast fast feedback for an agent. And as you can see by the process here that I'll walk you through in a second, this is not that fast. Uh, and so we have a fast gate that really double checks things really quickly to make sure that like things are in the right folders before we move on to our reviews.
</details>

实际上我们运行着六个审查器：两个代码审查器，分别运行在不同模型和不同模型家族上。嗯，目的是……那里的意图是切实发现……确保不会出现模型盲区，对吧？以免模型由于未在某些内容上训练过而导致我们没能捕获问题。这里就是我们使用 Anthropic 和 OpenAI 模型或者开源权重模型来作为那些杠杆的地方。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
We have six reviewers actually that run two code reviewers both in different models with different model families. Um, intended and the intent there is to really find to not make sure you don't have a model gap, right? So that the model wasn't trained on something that we don't catch. This is where we use the um anthropic and open AAI models or an openw weight model to be those uh levers.
</details>

我们还运行一个设计审查，询问：这个东西的形式是否正确？也就是说，我正在构建的内容——比如如果我在此 PR 中添加另一个缓存，我现有的缓存是否已经能解决这个问题？我们有一个第一性原理审查，询问：这次变更是否解决了真正的问题？嗯，我们同样还有一个 UX 审查，如果你在做一个……比方说你在新建一条错误提示信息，我们会让智能体根据已经发布的内容，来判断人类是否能够根据该错误信息知道该怎么做。接着，我们还在持续收紧安全态势。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
We run a design review that asks, does this thing have the right shape? Meaning, is what I'm building, like if I'm adding another cache in this PR, can the cache I already have take care of this issue or not? We have a first principles review that says, is this change addressing the real problem? Um, we have a UX review as well that if you're making a, let's say you're creating a new air message, we have an agent decide whether or not that air message, the human will know what to do with that air message based on what you we've published. And then we're consistently tightening our security posture.
</details>

现在，这些审查器每一个都拥有一票，非“是”即“否”，要么是红灯要么是绿灯。如果全部亮绿灯，它就进入合并。如果不是，我们就会进入仓库里的 prepare PR skill。如果大家想看，可以去查看这个 skill。然后那个 prepare PR skill 就会随着流程推进尝试去解决这些问题。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now each one of these reviewers gets one vote. It's either yes or no. It's red or it's green. If everything's green, it goes on to merge. If not, we go to a prepare PR skill that's in the repo. So you can check the skill out if you want. And that prepare PR skill then tries to resolve the issues as we go.
</details>

第三个教训——抱歉，关于审查还有一件事。正如我所说，我们很久之前就不再人工审查代码了

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The third lesson and sorry on the reviews. One more thing. Like I said, we've stopped reviewing code a long
</details>

<!-- chunk 21/52 -->

……很久以前的事了。我们已经往前走了，而我们所负责的并不是代码本身。看看这个能不能翻回去。确实是这样——我们所负责的是审查记录。它是否按照我们的预期遵循了流程，以及我们是否得到了想要的结果？如果没有，我们就改进审查流程。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
 time ago. We have moved on and what we're responsible for is not the code itself. See if this actually goes back. does what we were responsible for is the review record. Did it follow the process as we expected and did we get the results we wanted? If not, we fixed the review process.
</details>

另外还有两点。那么，呃，第一点是过度工程（overengineering）的问题。因此，我们有一个专门的规则来教导我们的智能体学会做减法，而不仅仅是做加法。所以，我们尽量在推进过程中从流程中移除代码，以防止那种过度工程。我们在路线图上也采用了同样的理念；在如今，说“好”正变得非常廉价，或者说已经变得非常廉价了，对吧？现在借助写代码，我们很容易就会说“好的，我会做那个，我把这些全做了”。而过去，我们必须对我们要构建什么非常挑剔。总是说“好”的问题在于，你可能会失去你的产品形态。我们已经在 Kira Kriet 中看到了这一点，它的发展如此迅速、节奏如此之快，以至于你不想让你的用户陷入杂音之中。所以再次重申，在路线图上使用同样的原则就是我们的做法。关于经验教训的最后一点是，合并 PR 并不意味着你就大功告成了，对吧？后面还有其他阶段。所以对我们来说，合并并不是我们所做的最后一项检查。相反，我们会运行一个发布门禁（release gate），智能体会下载代码仓库。它会安装它、运行它，然后检查核心流程，以确保整个系统在我们要进行发布之前仍然处于良好状态。接着我们也会一直进行安全检查。所以我们有，呃，每小时、每天以及常态化的——每小时对照我们的安全准则进行检查，每天对照应用安全（appsec）审查进行检查，然后还有一个常态化的渗透测试智能体。所以再次强调，合并并不是流程的终点。你必须把所有事情都压实。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Two other things. So, um, one, the overengineering problem. So, we have a specific role to teach our agents to subtract, not just add. And so, we try to remove code from the process as we go to prevent that overengineering. And we use that same philosophy on our road map as well, where saying yes is becoming very cheap or has become very cheap, right? And it's very easy for us now with code to say like yes, I'll do that and I'll do all of these things. Whereas we used to have to be very particular about what we were going to build. The problem with saying yes all the time is that you might lose your product shape. And we're already seeing this in in Kira Kriet that it's moving so fast and so quickly that you don't want your users to get caught in the noise. And so again, use the same principle on the road map is what we do. And then last thing on lessons is you're not done once you merge a PR, right? There's still other phases. And so for us merging is not the last check that we do. Instead we run a release gate where the agent will download the repo. It installs it, runs it and then checks the core flows to make sure that the overall system is still in good shape before we would do a release. And then we do security all the time as well. So we have uh every hour every day and always on every hour against our security guidance every day against an appseac review and then an always on pentest agent. So again merging is not the end of the process. You have to crunch everything down.
</details>

所以，我要回到我最初开始讲的地方。呃，你是否已经从你的智能体中获取了全部潜力？我们很多人都觉得我们目前还没有做到。而我今天在这里要提出、或者说我尝试提出的观点是：智能体在很久以前就已经足够快来处理这项工作了。而且它只会变得越来越好、越来越好。因此，我们一直在做的就是挑战我们的工作方式，并弄清楚我们究竟在哪里阻碍了智能体尽可能快地开展工作。我今天已经给大家举了无数个例子。再次说明，我在这里只提其中的几个。首先是我们的注意力跨度（attention span），对吧？当我充当反馈循环时，我就无法再进行交付了，因为我成了那个反馈循环。当我不在线时，智能体就停止工作了。笔记本电脑屏幕一盖上，你突然之间就失去了连接。迁移到远程可以解决这个问题，对吧？有着固定形态的固定流水线在事情稍微偏离轨道时无法应对。再次强调，你该如何解决这个问题？你——我们通过一个编排器解决了它。超级远程。还有无休止的审查——我想我讲这部分的深度可能已经超过了你们感兴趣的程度，但是呃，无休止的审查周期，以及那个只会做加法的循环，对吧？那个只往里面添加东西而不尝试做减法的循环。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So, I'm going to come back to where I started. Um, are you getting everything from your agent? And many of us don't feel like we are yet. And what I'm going to offer here today or what I've tried to offer is the agent was fast enough to work on this a long time ago. And it's only getting better and better and better. And so, what what we have been doing is challenging how we work and figuring out like where are we preventing the agent from working as quickly as possible. And I've given you numerous examples today. Again, I'll just call out a couple of them here. So, it started with our attention span, right? When I was the feedback loop, I could no longer ship because I was the feedback loop. When I wasn't there, the agent stopped working. The laptop lid closing, all of a sudden, you lose your connection. Moving to remote can solve that, right? The fixed pipeline with a fixed shape that can't respond when things go slightly off path. Again, how do you fix that? You we fixed it with an orchestrator. Ultra remote. the endless reviews that I think I've gone into probably more depth than you were interested in, but uh the endless review cycle and then the loop that only adds, right? The loop that only adds things to it and doesn't try to subtract.
</details>

最后我想留给大家的是，我也想明确一点，这将会是一个持续的调整，对吧？这并不是——这可能是在今天对我们有效的方式。我向你们保证，再过一个月，我们又会发现其他有效的方式，并且我们将继续大幅演进。但正如我在开头所说的，我们终于能够达到的交付速度，对于我们来说，我感觉终于在兑现 AI 的承诺了，但我仍然看到其他团队之间存在着差距。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And so I'll leave you with I also want to be clear that like this is going to be a continual uh adjustment, right? This is not like this may be what works for us today. I promise you in another month we're going to find something else that works and we're going to continue to evolve significantly. But like I said in the beginning, the pace that we're finally able to deliver at, I feel like for us is that finally we're we're meeting that promise of AI and and but I still see the disparity among other teams.
</details>

### 利用多智能体探索 Claude 前沿能力

好的，非常高兴能来到这里。呃，翻页器好用，电脑也正常。呃，我是来自 Anthropic 的 Lance。我知道我们在之前的一场主题演讲中遇到了一些问题，所以我们现在准备就绪了。我将讲述两个关于充分利用 Claude 以及探索 Claude 前沿能力的故事。首先是多智能体。这是我在 fable 52 发布时写的一篇博客，我想讲述一下这里面发生的故事。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
All right, it's very nice to be here. Um, the clicker works, the computer works. Uh, I'm Lance from Anthropic. I know we've had some issues with the one of the prior keynotes, so we're set to go. I'm going to tell two stories about getting the most out of Claude and exploring Claude's frontier capabilities. So, the first is multi-agent. This is a blog I wrote with the fable 52 launch and I want to kind of tell the story of what happened here.
</details>

费马大定理是数学界较为著名的断言之一。它大约在 350 年的时间里一直未被证明，而且它其实是一个非常简单的陈述：对于任何大于 2 的正整数，方程 $a^n + b^n = c^n$ 都没有解。然而，这个看似简单的陈述已被证明极难被真正证明。在 90 年代中期，安德鲁·怀尔斯爵士确实证明了它。呃，他发表了一份证明，这花了他整整七年的工作。过程极其艰辛。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So for math last theorem is one of the more kind of famous um claims in mathematics. It's stood and proven for around 350 years and it's actually a very simple statement just a to the n plus b to the n equals c to the n has no solution for any positive whole numbers greater than two. Now this deceptively simple kind of statement has proven to be quite difficult to actually prove. In the mid 90s, Sir Andrew Wilds uh did prove it. Uh he put out a proof and it took him seven years of work. It was extremely painstaking.
</details>

### 智能体记忆

现在我想转个方向，聊聊记忆（memory），这与我之前在多智能体方面所讲的完全不同，但它突出了我们看到 Claude 获得显著能力的另一个领域。之前我们差不多谈到了 Claude 编排大型团队以解决非常长程问题的能力。记忆则是一项不同的能力。它表明 Claude 越来越有能力组织这种上下文，以帮助解决长程问题。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now I want to shift gears and talk about memory which is uh entirely different when I talked about in terms of multi-aent but it's highlighting at another area where we're seeing claude gain significant capability. So previously we talked kind of about cloud's ability to orchestrate large teams to solve very long horizon problems. Memory is is a different capability. It's showing that claude is increasingly capable to organize this context to help solve long horizon problems.
</details>

我前不久就此发表了一篇博客，而这实际上是博客的开篇第一句话。我认为这实际上概括了该博客的想法，而且作为关于记忆的核心想法之一，我觉得非常有趣，这是来自哲学家克尔凯郭尔（Kierkegaard）：

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So I published an blog on this fairly recently and this is actually the lead line of the blog. I think this actually encapsulates the idea of the blog and like one of the central ideas around memory I think is very interesting and this is from the philosopher Kiergard.
</details>

生活只有往后看才能被理解，但必须往前过。这是智能体记忆的一个根本悖论，因为智能体写入记忆通常是在它们执行任务的过程中，遇到了奇怪或有趣的事情，就会写入记忆。但是记忆需要成为智能体知识的一张连贯地图，以便智能体可以回溯和反思。因此，当它们向记忆写入时，它们的写入是带有某种短视性的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So life can only be understood backwards but it must be lived forwards. This is a fundamental paradox of agent memory because agents write to memory typically as they go about a task they encounter something weird or interesting they write to memory. But memory needs to be a coherent map of an agent's knowledge that agents can look back on and reflect on. So when they're writing to memory, they're writing kind of myopically.
</details>

它们在一个 session 内贪婪地审视并想着：“啊，这对眼下的任务似乎很重要。”但是记忆需要是全局连贯且不冗余的。所以这是记忆的一个非常棘手的悖论。坦率地说，我们人类也面临着同样的挑战。我们在日常生活中活动，写入短期记忆。据信海马体大致就是负责编排此过程的地方。到了夜间，据信海马体会向存储长期知识的大脑新皮层进行回放。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
They're looking greedily within a session saying, "Ah, this seems important for this task at hand." But memory needs to be something that's globally coherent and not redundant. So this is like a very tricky paradox of memory. And frankly, we as humans have the same kind of challenge. We go about our day, we write to short-term memories. It's believed that the hippocampus kind of is the place that that's orchestrated. At night, it's believed that the hippocampus replays to the neoortexes where long-term knowledge is stored.
</details>

呃，因此人们认为，人类的这种做梦过程实际上有助于将我们日常经历的碎片或最重要的方面巩固整合为长期知识。因此，在日常记忆和某种长期知识之间存在这种解耦。我们发现智能体也能从同样的解耦中获益。这确实是该文章的核心要点。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Uh, so it's believed that actually this dreaming process for humans help consolidate the fragments or like the most important aspects of our daily experience into long-term knowledge. So there's this decomposition between daily memory and kind of long-term knowledge. And we found agents can benefit from the same decomposition. That's really the key point of the post.
</details>

现在，这里有一些说明情况的结果。这实际上是一个名为 CLB bench 的开源持续学习基准测试，它表明随着你从 Sonnet 一路往上到像 Open 5 这样的不同模型，它们在使用记忆方面变得越来越出色。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>

</details>

好的，同样值得注意的是，这些实验是在没有特定记忆 prompt 的情况下完成的。它只是告诉智能体该目录可用，如果想用的话可以使用记忆。其实仅此而已。因此 Sonnet 46 原生上无法有效地使用记忆。Sonnet 5 确实表现更好，Opus 也更好，所以基本上模型在相同的简单指令下，使用记忆的能力变得越来越强。

Okay, it's also worth noting that these experiments are done with no specific memory prompting. It just tells the agent the directory is available use memory if you want. That's really it. 而这里真正的核心观点是，更强的模型所表现出的是，它们内在地知道何时向记忆写入，并且它们内在也大致知道何时从记忆中读取以检索信息。它们的记忆也更好。它们就像是高度经过验证的陈述，或者是关于数据库状态或接下来可能发生什么情况的预测，相比之下，较早的模型往往会写下质量较低的记忆。

And really the key idea here is that what happens is with stronger models they know intrinsically when to write to memory and they kind of know intrinsically uh when to read from memory to retrieve information. They're like they're highly verified statements or predictions about kind of the state of the database or what may happen next versus earlier models tend to write um lower quality memories.
</details>

游戏进行得远不如 Fable 5。Fable 5 的记忆目录紧凑得多。它包含的完全是战略洞见、战术知识，是那些你无法轻易从游戏状态中直接获得的东西。所以我们看到的是，更优秀的模型具有更好的显著性直觉，更清楚该往记忆中写入什么。你可以在记忆目录的大小上看到这一点反映出来。它紧凑得多，战略性强得多，而且 Fable 5 在游戏过程中确实比 Sonic 37 走得远得多。这从另一个角度印证了一个直觉：Claude 在写入记忆方面表现好得多，清楚该写什么、什么是显著的，而且在利用和读取记忆方面也是如此。因此模型在感知显著性、知晓写什么以及读取记忆方面正变得越来越有效。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>gameplay and it proceeds much worse in the game than Fable 5. Fable 5's memory directory is much tighter. It's really strategic insights, tactical knowledge, things you can't just get from game state very easily. So what we see is better models are much are have much better instincts for salience and like what to write to memory. And you can see this reflected in the size of the memory directories. It's much tighter. It's much more strategic and gameplay indeed with Fable 5 gets much further than Sonic 37. So it gives another intuition around the fact that Claude is much better at writing to memory, knowing kind of what to write in salience and what's salient and uh and also kind of using and reading from memory. So models are getting more and more u kind of effective at knowing salience, knowing what to write and also reading.</details>

现在，这两种情况都属于我在最初框架中所说的在经历过程中写入记忆。基本上，在这两项任务中，我们都让 Claude 在玩这个游戏时写入记忆。我们让 Claude 在运行 Sealbench 基准测试时写入记忆。但是正如我提到的，记忆还有梦境这一面。这能让 Claude 对记忆进行反思，从而修正问题。我会展示一些这方面的有趣示例。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Now, both of these represent what I would consider in my initial framing of like writing to memory during experience. So, basically in both of these tasks, we let Claude write to memory while it plays this game. We let Claude write to memory while it does the Sealbench benchmark. But, as I mentioned, there's also the dreaming side of memory. So, this is allowing Claude to reflect over memories to fix things. And I'll show some interesting examples of that.</details>

### 梦境机制与记忆构建

这是关于梦境的一种构想框架。你可以把记忆理解为……嗯，存在转录记录。转录记录可以被视为记忆的基石。想象一下，一个记忆系统可以非常简单，仅仅是转录记录搜索。Claude 在执行某项任务时写入转录记录。未来的会话可以从转录记录中读取。当然，转录记录体量庞大，非常笨重。我们在博文中反复谈到这一事实：转录记录中通常包含的信息……你知道，转录记录中其实只有极少极少的一部分信息是显著且重要的。因此我们也允许 Claude 执行诸如撰写记忆笔记这样的操作。这就是我刚才谈到的内容。随着任务推进，它可以向记忆写入笔记。但梦境能让你做得更多。梦境让你能够对转录记录以及这些写下的笔记进行反思，并重新表征它们。例如，构建像 wiki 这样的记忆表征。甚至可以将某些记忆提升为注入式上下文，并预先加载到未来的每一个会话中。所以梦境是一种机制，通过它你可以处理现有的转录记录或现有的记忆笔记，并从中构建新的东西，包括表征、注入式上下文等。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>This is kind of a a framing of dreaming. And so you can think about memory in terms of um there are transcripts. Transcripts you can think about as kind of the foundation of memory. So imagine a memory system could very simply be just transcript search. Claude writes to a transcript as it goes about a task. Future session can read from the transcript. Of course transcripts are large. It's very unwieldy. We talk a lot about in the blog post the fact that transcripts often contain information that you know a very very small amount of information in transcripts is actually salient and matters. So we also allow claw to do things like writing memory notes. That's what I just talked about. As it goes, it can write notes to memory. But dreaming lets you do more. Dreaming lets you reflect over transcripts and these written notes and re re-represent them. Build memory representations like wikis as an example. Actually promote certain memories to injected context and load it preemptively into every future session. So memory is this dreaming is this mechanism where you can kind of process existing transcripts or existing memory notes and build new things from them including representations, injected contexts and more.</details>

来看一些这方面的有趣示例，这是来自《宝可梦》游戏的实机情况。梦境是纠正记忆中错误的有效途径。正如我前面谈到的，当 Claude 执行任务时，它可能会在记忆中写下不正确的笔记。就是它犯了个错。这发生在这场特定的游戏中。嗯，Claude 错误地记下了游戏中某个地标的位置。伴随着这条错误写入的记忆，发生的情况是 Claude 陷入了一个死循环：基本上它会在游戏的这个特定节点误判自身位置，掉进这个活板门陷阱，然后基本上被送回上一层。这就是大体上的逻辑。具体细节并没有那么有趣或重要。关键思想在于，一条有毒的记忆被注入并卡在了那里。梦境能够化解这一困境。这就是为什么梦境在对记忆进行反思和修正方面极其强大的直观原因。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So some fun examples of this, this is from Pokemon gameplay. Dreaming is an effective way to correct mistakes in memory. So as I talked about when Claude's going about a task, it can write incorrect notes to memory. Just it makes a mistake. This happened this is in in this particular game. um Claude wrote incorrectly wrote the location uh of a particular landmark in the game. Now what happened is with this incorrectly written memory, Claude got trapped in a loop where basically it would misloize itself at this particular point in the game, fall down this trap door and it would basically send it back to the prior level. That's like the highle intuition. The details aren't that interesting or important. The key idea is a poison memory was injected and it's stuck there. Dreaming can rescue that. That's really the intuition on why dreaming is extremely powerful for reflecting over memories and correcting them.</details>

这里展示了游戏的一些运行轨迹，其中橙色代表记忆与梦境。向上代表好的态势。基本上，向上代表游戏进展，向下代表某种游戏倒退。有趣的是，对于原始记忆，嗯，基本上在没有梦境的情况下，我们可以看到它崩溃了——植入了一条错误的记忆，而在到达那个特定节点时，Claude 在游戏中继续倒退或跌落；而有了梦境，它化解了那个错误，Claude 推进到了下一层。这确实是关键的观察结论，而且这是一个非常通用的原理：Claude 可以利用梦境来修正记忆中原本卡在那里的错误。这是一种能够纠正错误的重要途径。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So this is showing some trajectories of the game where orange is memory and dreaming. Up is good. So basically up represents game progress and down represents kind of game regression. And what's interesting is raw memory um basically without dreaming we can see crashes out a incorrect memory is planted and clock continues to basically regress or fall in the game when it hits that particular point with dreaming it rescues that mistake claw proceeds to the next level. That's really the key observation and this is a very general principle that claw can use dreaming to correct mistakes in memory that are otherwise stuck there. It's a very important way you can kind of um kind of correct from errors.</details>

### 构建记忆表征：Wiki

这是在记忆中构建表征的另一个有趣例子。大家对将 wiki 作为 Claude 组织知识的通用方式表现出了浓厚兴趣。这是一个非常有趣的示例：我们在五月份收购了一家名为 Stainless 的公司，我们获得了该公司的语料库，包含其所有文档、Slack 消息等。我们对内容进行了去匿名化……我们对内容进行了匿名化处理，并基于它构建了一个数据集，用来测试梦境。我们所做的是创建了一个由 25 个关于该公司的问题组成的评估集，这些问题只能根据该语料库来回答。我们允许 Claude 根据这些信息构建一个 wiki，以帮助它回答问题。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Now, this is another fun example of building representations in memory. So, there's been a lot of interest in wikis as kind of general um kind of ways that cloud can organize knowledge. This is a really fun example uh from we acquired a company Stainless back in May and we had access to the the company Corpus all its documents and Slack messages and so forth and we dean anonym we anonymize things and we built um kind of a a data set from it which allowed us to test dreaming. So what we did was we created kind of an email set of 25 questions about the company that can only be answered from this corpus. We allow Claude to build a wiki from this information to help it answer questions.</details>

你可以看到，我们对比了一个原始搜索工具，它只是对这类文档进行某种语义搜索。我们将其作为基准进行比较，它的表现相当不错。你可以看到它达到了大约 53%。仅仅是搜索。我们还测试了让 Claude 在这套知识库上构建一个 wiki。你可以看到，随着你推进、投入越来越多的梦境 token，wiki 变得越来越好、越来越完善，使 Claude 能够回答问题。因此，Claude 原生具备随着时间推移，通过 wiki 逐步构建出对该知识语料库的表征的能力。随着你增加更多梦境计算，wiki 变得越来越有用。我们认为，对于思考智能体如何随时间推移构建知识，这里有一个非常好的心智模型：可以赋予它们访问文本转录记录原始语料库的权限，它们能够构建这些表征，以便更轻松地搜索这些转录文本来回答问题，并以有效的方式连接它们的知识。我们还发现，将 wiki 与搜索工具结合使用，实际上比单纯的原始搜索更进一步提升了效果。所以这带来了另一个直观认识：基本上 Claude 也可以在其记忆中构建像 wiki 这样的表征，以帮助它解决问题。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>What you see is we compare a raw search tool that's just like some kind of semantic search over this this kind of documentation. We compare that as a baseline which does reasonably well. You can see it's like 53%. Just for search. We also test letting quad build a wiki over this knowledge. And you can see as you push, you add more and more dreaming tokens, the wiki gets better and better and better allowing Claude to answer questions. So Claude natively can kind of build up over time a representation of this of this kind of corpus of knowledge through a wiki. And as you add more dreaming kapu, the wiki gets more and more useful. And we think there's a really nice mental model to think about how agents can kind of build knowledge over time. they can be given access to kind of raw corpuses of texts of transcripts and they can kind of build these representations that allow them to search those tonic texts much more easily to answer questions and to connect their knowledge in effective ways. We also find that adding the wiki and the search tool together actually bumps further beyond just raw search. So it's kind of another intuition that basically claude can also build representations like wikis in its kind of memory um in order to help it kind of solve problems.</details>

最后一个例子来自一个不同的基准测试，但这里展示的是 Claude 构建的两个不同的 wiki。这是在梦境处理之前和梦境处理之后的对比。我们看到的是，在此特定案例中，当 Claude 构建 wiki 时存在一些空白，例如这个初始 wiki 包含了一堆针对不存在页面的占位符。当 Claude 构建各种记忆表征时可能会发生这种情况。如果缺少梦境计算，可能就没有机会去挽救或修复这些错误。我们观察到的是，当我们运行梦境时，梦境可以审视 Claude 现有的表征（例如 wiki），并能够填补疏漏。因此在这个案例中，它填补了许多缺失的空白。它创建了一个索引，因此再次证明，梦境是 Claude 进行反思、并在其记忆中投入计算以实现提升的一种方式——可以通过像我谈到的那样纠正错误，通过像我们在这里看到的填补其知识空白，或者通过创建像 wiki 这样的新表征来实现。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And a final example, this is from a different benchmark uh but um this is showing two different wiks that claude built. So this is pri prior to dreaming and after dreaming. What we saw is when claude was building wiks in this particular case, there were some gaps and for example this initial wiki had a bunch of placeholders for pages that didn't exist. This can happen when cla's kind of building different memory representations. And without dreaming compute, there may not be opportunities to rescue or fix those errors. that we saw is when we ran dreaming, dreaming can look over Claude's existing representation like for example wiki and can fill in mistakes. So in this case it fills in a lot of missing gaps. It creates an index and so again dreaming is a way that claude can reflect and and kind of apply compute across its memory to improve it by correcting mistakes like I talked about by filling in gaps in its knowledge like we see here uh or for example by creating new representations like wikis.</details>
