### 最佳 Prompt 与隐式状态机

我们来梳理一下。呃，我们先来看第一个小环节，第一条新闻。他们称之为有史以来最棒的 prompt。这条推文最近在网上疯传，因为这确实是一个很好的 prompt：“找出我应用中所有隐式的状态机（implicit state machines）。”CJ，为什么这是一个好 prompt？你用过这个 prompt 吗？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>let's break it down here. Um, let's get into our first little bit, the first bit of news. They're calling it the greatest prompt ever. So, this tweet has been going viral here because this is a good prompt. Uh, find all implicit state machines in my app. CJ, why is this a good prompt? Have you used this prompt?</details>

我没用过这个具体的 prompt，但我用过类似的。而且我认为这个 prompt 后面应该还有一个后续 prompt，因为隐式状态机本质上就是你没有显式定义的状态机，对吧？你的代码里散落着一堆变量，比如 `isLoading` 或者 `isRunning`，然后你在各处写满了针对这些变量的代码和 if 语句。所以这个 prompt 本质上就像是：让我们把那些面条代码（spaghetti code）转变成更容易推导理解的东西。对吧。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>I haven't used this prompt, but I've used similar prompts. And I think that like there would be a followup to this prompt because uh in and an implicit state machine is essentially a state machine that you haven't defined, right? So you have a bunch of variables all over your code like is loading or uh is running and then you have code and if statements all over those. And so this prompt is essentially like let's take that spaghetti code and turn it into something that uh is easier to reason about. Right.</details>

>> 是的。是的。所以基本上，如果你不知道什么是隐式状态机或显式状态机，最简单的理解方式就是：隐式状态机就是你以前在各种 React 代码库里经常看到被讨论的那些东西。像 `isLoading`、`hasError`、`isSuccess` 这些。大家以前常常把它们做成布尔状态，对吧？像这样。而这带来的大问题是，它容易出 bug、容易出错，而且还可能把你的 UI 置于不可能的状态（impossible states）。所以理论上，如果它们只是两个独立的布尔值，你可能会遇到 `isLoading` 和 `isError` 同时为 true 的情况，那这时候你是要把两者都显示出来，还是只显示其中一个或另一个？所以“找出我应用中所有隐式状态机”这个 prompt 的思路，就是你能把它们转换成显式状态机。显式状态机有声明好的状态。它们拥有的是具体的值而不是布尔标志位，对吧？并且它们具有状态转移，对吧，带有集中式的规则。所以如果你用过像 Redux 这样的东西——虽然它曾经一度……

<details><summary>Original English</summary>>> Yes. Yes. So basically, if you don't know what an implicit state machine or an explicit state machine is, the easiest way to think about this is that an implicit state machine is stuff that you used to see talked about all over React code bases. Stuff like is loading, has error, is success. Uh people these are often like a a boolean state, right? Like this. And the big problem with that is is that it can be bugprone, it could be errorprone, but it can also put your UI into impossible states. So the the idea with this prompt find all implicit state machines in my app is that you are able to convert them to explicit state machines. Explicit state machines have a declared status. Uh they they have a value rather than a boolean flag, right? And they have state transitions, right, with centralized rules. >> 没错。就我个人而言，我发现 lint 规则在处理这方面非常管用。比如，一个例子是我正在构建的一个 CLI 工具，它涉及到大量的文件读写。所以它会在你系统的各处写入文件并保存它们。在重构之前，它大概有 10 个不同的地方用了 10 种不同的方式来写入文件。但后来我把它们全部整合进了一个单独的文件管理器中，我的代码中任何想要写文件的部分都必须使用这个文件管理器。所以就有一条 lint 规则写着：禁止裸调 `fs.read`，禁止裸调 `fs.write`。这基本上就强制了 AI 必须使用那个抽象。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>>> exactly. And personally, I've found like lint rules to be really good for that. So like uh one example is a a CLI tool that I'm building that does a lot of file read and writes. So it's writing files all over your system. It's saving them. And before I refactored, it had like 10 different places with 10 different ways of writing the file. But then I brought it all into a single file manager and any part of my code that wants to write a file has to use the file manager. So there's a lint rule that says no bare fs read, no bare fs write and that basically forces the AI to use that abstraction.</details>

>> 完全赞同。是的。所以我很喜欢这个 prompt，也喜欢这类事情。因此，找出隐式状态机并将它们改成显式状态机，并在脑海中切实掌控好应用的状态，在我看来确实能够降低代码库的劣质程度（slop level）。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>>> Totally. Yeah. So uh I I I like this prompt and I like this type of thing. So finding your implicit state machines and changing them to be explicit state machines and like really getting a handle on the state of your application in my in my mind is something that does decrease the slop level of a code base.</details>

### 技能演变与 AI 时代的“脑腐”

嗯，至于这种“脑腐”（思维退化）到底发生在哪里，我认为它存在于多个层面。很多其实源于管理层，比如管理层会要求“使用 AI，加快进度”，然后开发者就不得不紧跟节奏，接着他们就会精疲力竭，以至于现在他们甚至都顾不上担心自己是否还懂得写代码了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um in terms of where the brain rot is happening like I think it's at multiple levels though because a lot of it comes from management like management is like use AI move fast and then developers are having to stay caught up and then they're burning out and so now they can't even worry about uh do they still know how to code.
</details>

>> 嗯，所以这种情况确实在发生，但我认为这全都是这场大转变的一部分，就像是我们在朝哪里走、我们正走向何方？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
>> Um so it's happening but it's all I think part of this big shift of like where where do we where are we going where are we headed?
</details>

>> 是的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
>> Yes.
</details>

>> 太棒了。好的，我们来看下一个问题。能不能谈谈在 2026 年 vibe coding 究竟意味着什么？作为一名中级开发者，我看到大多数同行（如果不是全部的话）都在使用 AI，因为不用它就根本无法竞争。但对许多人来说，vibecoded（vibe 编码生成的）现在成了一个贬义词。哇。是的。有时它会被不公平地扣在纯手写项目、有经验开发者借助 AI 完成的项目上，而用在非程序员使用昂贵模型做出的东西上则是名副其实的。那么……

### 数据流水线与 AI 评审

想象一个 Pull Request。它修改了我们其中一条数据流水线中的模式定义（schema）。它在 CI 中显示为绿色，测试全部通过，linter 检查合格，关于它的一切看起来都很干净。

那么，合并它是否安全？因为像这样的更改仍然可能破坏下游的某些东西。也许它破坏了新鲜度 SLA，或者也许破坏了隔了三个团队、对此毫不知情的下游消费者。又或者也许它暴露了本不该被访问的数据。CI 通过与这个变更实际上安全之间的差距，正是我的团队每天必须去弥合的鸿沟。大家好，我是 Jas。我是 Robinhood 数据平台团队的一名工程师。我设计并构建了我们数据湖背后的分布式数据摄入和处理框架，全公司的各个团队都依赖这些框架来利用数据做出决策。

AI 编程助手让提交 Pull Request 变得成本极低。一个新的查询、一次 schema 变更或一次权限授予，现在只需要几分钟。而且助手提交 Pull Request 的速度，远远快于任何人类团队所能达到的速度。

我今天谈论的 PR，是指针对我们数据流水线的 PR，比如 schema 变更、流水线代码、配置变更，以及授予某人数据访问权限的授权。

数据平台是负责评审这类 PR 的团队。因此，当这些 PR 积压起来时，就全堆积在了我们身上。而我即将描述的评审器，正是我们为了处理它们而构建的一个系统。因此，感受到这种痛点的是一小部分人：掌握着能够判断合并到生产环境中是否安全这一上下文的工程师，以及所有等待他们的人。

那么为什么这是个问题呢？由 AI 提交的 PR 源源不断地到来。平心而论，我们已有的安全网是有效的。CI 检查与人工评审相结合，一直以来都能发现许多真正的错误，比如损坏的代码、低效的查询，以及诸如下游损坏或敏感数据泄露等风险。但是人类这一半只能以人类的速度运作，并且只能依靠某个人恰好印在脑子里的上下文。谁在读取这张表？它有多大？里面的数据有多敏感？PR 正在以机器速度涌入，但是理解合并到生产数据流水线中是否安全的人员数量，却根本没有增加。这就是不匹配之处。队列在少数拥有上下文的人面前积压起来，问题正是从这里开始滑坡。

太慢或太昂贵。因此，CI 性能测试使用模拟数据，在暂存环境中以与生产环境相同的数据量运行该变更。[清嗓子] 这在 PR 合并之前告诉我们两件事：集群规模是否适合该作业，以及该作业是否仍能满足其新鲜度 SLA。那些正是否则会在几周后作为故障显现出来的问题。所以这就是第一层检查的完整集合。让我们看看它们对我们的 PR 怎么说。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
be too slow or too expensive. So the CI performance test runs the change in staging at the same data volume as [clears throat] production using mock data. So that tells us two things before the PR merges. Whether the cluster is sized right for the job and whether the job will still make its freshness SLA. Those are the kind of problems that otherwise show up weeks later as an incident. So that's the full set of layer 1 checks. Let's see what they said about our PR.
</details>

回到我们正在运行的 PR。和之前一样的画面。以下是第一层对其的说明。

</details>

The data contract check went to the schema registry and asked whether the new column really exists upstream as registered. It does and the check passes.
</details>

就脚本所能看到的而言，这个 PR 是安全的。但我仔细推敲了这些词句。就脚本所能看到的而言，幻灯片上的所有内容都是关于已注册画面的陈述。我们的系统记录在案的作业和列。请记住这一点。这并不是全貌。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
As far as the scripts can see, this PR is safe. But I chose those words carefully. As far as the scripts can see, everything on the slide is a statement about the registered picture. the jobs and the columns our systems have a record of. And keep that in mind. It is not the whole story.
</details>

### 代码审查 Agent 与代码遍历

现在，让我们看看带有 diff 的 Agent。究竟改变了什么？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now, let's look at the agent with the diff. What actually changed?
</details>

PR 描述和提交信息解释了作者的意图，但意图绝不能开脱真正的发现。如果描述说它是安全的，而代码显示并非如此，以代码为准。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The PR description and the commit messages explain the author's intent, but intent never explains away a real finding. If the description says it is safe and the code says otherwise, the code wins.
</details>

文件工具允许 Agent 读取、搜索和列出整个代码仓库中的文件。它追踪 diff 之外的变更，深入到流水线定义和访问授权中，寻找作者可能忽略的关系。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
File tools let the agent read, search and list the files across the repository. It follows changes beyond the diff into pipeline definitions and access grants looking for the relationships the author may have missed.
</details>

这些仅是代码仓库工具。Agent 无法查询实时元数据目录、血缘工具或生产状态。实时事实仍然是第一层的工作。Agent 对代码进行推理。那么为什么要针对模式变更去遍历那些代码呢？因为已注册的血缘并不是全貌。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
These are the repository repository tools only. The agent cannot query the live catalog, lineage tool or production state. Life facts stay layer 1's job. The agent reasons over the code. So why walk that code for a schema change? Because registered lineage is not the whole picture.
</details>

规则定义了那种判断的归属。它们根据 PR 中的变更类型进行配置，已知第一层已经涵盖的内容。它们针对查询反模式、敏感数据索取以及血缘之外的消费方。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The rules define where that judgment belongs. They are configured by the change type in the PR knowing what layer 1 already covers. They target query antiatterns, sensitive data asks and consumers outside lineage.
</details>

例如，一个未分区的交叉连接可以正确运行，但仍然会冲击集群。一个授权看起来可能很寻常，但仍然会开放敏感数据。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
For example, an unpartitioned cross join can run correctly and still hammer a cluster. A grant can look ordinary and still open sensitive data.
</details>

对于模式变更，Agent 遍历代码仓库中的流水线定义和访问授权，以查找未注册的消费方。这是一项繁琐的搜索，嗯，否则需要人工手动完成。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And for the schema changes, the agent walks the pipeline definitions and access grants in the repository to find consumers that were not registered. It's a tedious search uh a person would otherwise do by hand.
</details>

让我们回到我们的 PR，看看那项搜索发现了什么。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Let's return to our PR and see what that search found.
</details>

回到我们正在运行的 PR。同样的画面。第一层表示加宽列的每个已注册消费方看起来都很好。现在 Agent 遍历代码，

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So back to our running PR. Same picture. Layer one said every registered consumer of the vid column looks fine. Now the agent walks the code
</details>

它发现了一个血缘从未知道的作业。它就在代码仓库里。它从我们的宽表中读取我们加宽的列，但它从未在血缘中注册过，这正是第一层检查无法看到它的原因。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
and it finds a job that lineage never knew about. It's right there in the repository. It reads our widened column from our late table but it was never registered in the lineage which is exactly why the layer 1 check could not see it.
</details>

该作业获取该值并将其写入另一个表。而在那另一个表中，该列仍然是整数。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And that job takes the value and writes it into another table. And in that other table, the column is still an integer.
</details>

现在顺着推演下去。我们的列是一个大整数。现在，第一次传来一个大于整数所能容纳的值时，该写入就会失败。一个有效的模式链，在 CI 中全绿，通过了每一项已注册的检查，但下游作业仍然中断。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now follow it through. Our column is a big integer. Now the first time a value comes through that's bigger than an integer can hold, that write fails. A valid schema chain green in CI passes every registered check and a downstream job still breaks.
</details>

Agent 在其审查评论中准确标记了这一链路并附带了推理依据。这样人就可以看到从我们的列到失败写入的完整路径。我们稍后会讲到接下来发生了什么。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The agent flagged exactly this chain in its review comment with the reasoning. So a person could see the whole path from our column to the failing right. We'll get to what happened next in a moment.
</details>

那么为什么血缘会漏掉那个作业呢？因为企业数据世界是混乱的。工作负载运行在不同的查询引擎上，每个引擎都必须发布自己的血缘。如果有一个不发布，该关系就会从画面中缺失。并不存在能自动看到一切的单一目录。所以已注册的画面是有用的，但它并不是全貌。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So why did the lineage miss that job? Because the enterprise data world is messy. Workloads run across different query engines and each engine has to publish its own lineage. If one does not publish, that relationship is missing from the picture. There's a single catalog that automatically sees everything. So the registered picture is useful but it is not the whole picture.
</details>

现在我不想过分夸大代码遍历。它确实有用，但它无法看到一切。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now I don't want to oversell the codewalk. It's genuinely useful but it cannot see everything.
</details>

Agent 可以搜索代码仓库中的内容。流水线代码、访问授权，以及其中定义的读取我们表的作业。那就是它找到我们未注册作业的地方。但大量的数据使用存在于代码库之外。有人手动运行的即席查询。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The agent can search what is in the repo. Pipeline code access grants and the jobs defined there that read our tables. That's where it found our unregistered job. But plenty of data use lives outside the code base. Ad hawk queries someone runs by hand.
</details>

仪表板，这些对于代码仓库搜索来说是完全隐形的。无论 Agent 多么仔细地寻找，外面的消费方永远不会显现。所以我们让 Agent 诚实面对这种限制。如果它没有找到任何东西，它不能说不存在下游消费方。它会说明在它搜索过的地方没有找到。没有找到并不等同于不存在。而且 Agent 永远不能宣称彻底排除隐患。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Dashboards, those are completely invisible to a repository search. A consumer out there never shows up no matter how carefully the agent looks. So we make the agent honest about that limit. If it does not find anything, it does not get to say no downstream consumers exist. It says it found none in the places it searched. None found is not the same as none exist. And the agent never gets to claim an all clear.
</details>

### 审查产出与人工协同

那么 Agent 遍历了代码。它实际上产出什么？一份审查。就像优秀的真人审阅者会给你的形式一样。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So the agent has walked the code. What does it actually produce? A review. The same shape a good human reviewer would give you.
</details>

首先，针对关键具体行的审查评论。每一条都带有其推理依据。不是含糊的“看起来有风险”，而是“这一行修改了这一列，这里是读取它的作业”。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
First review comments on the specific lines that matter. Each with its reasoning. Not a vague looks risky, but this line changes this column. And here is the job that reads it.
</details>

其次，风险评分。衡量它所发现问题的严重程度。正是这一点驱动了下一步。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Second, a risk score. How severe is what it found. That's what drives the next step.
</details>

然后它做出裁决。如果没有严重风险，它可以批准。如果存在需要人介入的风险，它会升级给人工，评论和推理依据也一并转交。这样人就有了上下文去调查，而不必从头开始。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Then it makes a call. If there is no serious risk, it can approve. If there is a risk that needs a person, it escalates to a human and the comments and reasoning go with it. So the person has the context to investigate instead of starting from scratch.
</details>

明确说明合并需要满足的条件：第一层的检查必须通过，且审查必须批准——要么由 Agent 批准，要么在升级后由人工批准。双方都必须同意。现在让我们看看我们的 PR 是怎样的情况。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And to be clear about what it takes to merge, layer 1's checks have to pass and the review has to approve either the agent or after an escalation a person. Both have to say yes. Now let's see what that looked like for our PR.
</details>

这就是我们正在运行的 PR 实际的结局。同样的画面。已注册的消费方看起来没问题，而代码遍历发现了一个未注册的作业，该作业将我们加宽的列写入一个其类型仍为整数的表中

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Here is how our running PR actually ended. Same picture. The registered consumers look fine and the core walk found an unregistered job that writes our widened column into a table where it is still an integer
</details>

因为 Agent 发现了一个真正的下游故障。它评定风险为高，并升级给人工，在审查评论中阐明了整个链路。现在由人工来完成 Agent 无法完成的部分。Agent 仅对代码仓库进行推理。它无法看到该作业是否实际上正在生产环境中运行。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
because the agent found a real downstream break. It scored the risk high and escalated to a human with the whole chain spelled out in its review comment. Now the human does the part the agent cannot. The agent only reasons over the repo. It cannot see whether the job is actually running in production.
</details>

人工审阅者进行了检查，并确认 Agent 发现的消费方是实时且活跃的。所以这不是一个理论上的风险。PR 进行了等待

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
human reviewer checked and confirmed that the consumer the agent found was live and active. So this was not a theoretical risk. The PR waited
</details>

修复的关键在于顺序，而不是拒绝变更。下游表首先被更新，使其列能够容纳更宽的类型，只有在那之后，我们的 PR 才进行合并和部署。没有发生故障。第一层中的脚本显示安全。Agent 发现了漏洞，而人工做出了决断。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
the fisk the fix was about order not about rejecting the change. The downstream table was updated first so its column could handle the wider type and only then did our PR merge and deploy. No incident. The scripts in layer 1 said safe. The agent found the gap and a human made the call.
</details>

这并不是一次性的事情。大多数变更都可以由 Agent 放行。当检查通过且 Agent 对其结论确信时，它会自行批准。但有一整类变更，Agent 永远不能给予批准。无论它听起来多么自信，

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And that was not a one-time thing. Most changes can be cleared by the agent. When the checks pass and the agent is confident in its verdict, it approves on its own. But there's a whole category of changes where the agent never gets to say approved. And no matter how confident it sounds,
</details>

涉及敏感数据的授权就是其中之一。谁能访问敏感数据，是我们希望每次都由人工把关的决定。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
grants touching sensitive data are one. Who can reach sensitive data is a decision we want a person to own every time.
</details>

看起来改变了意图而不仅仅是机制的迁移，是另一种情况。如果一个变更改变了数据的含义，而不仅仅是其移动或存储方式，由人来决定。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
A migration that looks like it changes intent, not just mechanics, is another. If a change alters what the data means rather than just how it is moved or stored, a person decides.
</details>

如果 Agent 本身对自己的结论没有把握，那是第三种情况。它不能仅仅因为没有查出具体错误就四舍五入直接批准。在这全部三种情况下，它的工作仅仅是指出风险及其推理依据。决定权依然在人工手中。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And if the agent itself is not confident in its own verdict, that's the third. It does not get to round up to approve just because nothing concrete came back wrong. In all the all the three cases, his job is only ever to surface the risk and his reasoning. The decision stays with a person.
</details>

这是一个 PR。现在，这如何在每个 PR 中保持一致，审阅者又是如何随着时间的推移变得越来越好的？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
That is one PR. Now, how does this hold up across every PR and how does the reviewer get better over time?
</details>

我们如何知道标记是否有用，或者规则变更是否有帮助？我们从审查评论中 Agent 的结论及其推理依据开始。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
How do we know the flags are useful or whether a rule change helps? We start with the agents verdict and its reasoning in the review comments.
</details>

人工回复表示同意或不同意，并解释原因。正是这种解释使得反馈具有实用价值，而不仅仅是一次投票。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
A person replies agreeing or disagreeing and explain why. That explanation is what makes the feedback useful rather than just a vote.
</details>

这些回复每天都会被收集。值班人员每周阅读一次反馈，并手动更新审查规则。这些规则指导下一次审查。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Those replies are collected every day. Once a week, the on call reads the feedback and updates the review rules by hand. Those rules shape the next review.
</details>

我知道这目前是人工的。没有任何东西会自动重新训练，也没有规则会自动更改。但我们有一个想法，让事故调查 Agent 协助进行每周的检查。但它还没有构建出来。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
I know this is manual today. Nothing retrains itself and no rule changes automatically. But we have an idea to let an incident investigation agent help with that weekly pass. But it is not built.
</details>

如果没有反馈，每一次规则变更都将是一次猜测。那么让我向你们展示一个例子，其中

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Without the feedback, every rule change would be a guess. So let me show you an example where a
</details>

<!-- chunk 31/52 -->

人工纠正了智能体，而我们利用该反馈来改进它的下一次审查。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
human corrected the agent and we use that feedback to improve its next review.
</details>

这里有一个真实的例子。注意角落里的标签。这是一个单独的例子，不是我们的模式变更 PR。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So here is a real one. And note the label in the corner. This is a separate example, not our schema change PR.
</details>

在源数据库中，某一列被标记为个人信息。但当它存入数据湖时，摄取管道已经对其进行了加密。因此该 PR 提议在数据湖中将其标记为非敏感，尽管上游数据仍然是 PII。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
A column was marked as personal information in the source database. But by the time it lands in the data lake, the injection pipeline has already encrypted it. So the PR proposed labeling it nonsensitive in the data lake even though the upstream data is still PII.
</details>

智能体对此提出了警告。从它的角度看，上游标记为 BII，所以非敏感标签看起来是不对的。作者提出异议，并要求智能体去查看其他表上的类似列之前是如何分类的，而不是单纯依据上游的标签。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The agent flagged this. From where it stood, the upstream says BII, so a nonsensitive label looked wrong. The author pushed back and asked the agent to look at how similar columns on the other tables were already classified instead of going purely off the upstream tagging.
</details>

一位人工审查员同意了作者的看法。写入前的加密使得该列标记为非敏感看起来是可行的。这种纠正正是让规则变得更聪明的信号，因为下一次出现类似列时，这一上下文就能阻止一次不必要的呈报升级。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
A human agreed with the author. The encryption before write made the nonsensitive label look okay for this column. And that kind of correction is exactly the signal that teaches the rules to get smarter because the next time a similar column comes through that context can stop a needless escalation.
</details>

不过需要提个醒：这取决于具体管道在写入前进行了加密。并不是说“加密总是会让数据变成非敏感”这样一条一概而论的规则。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
One caution though, this depends on the specific pipeline encrypting before the right. It's not a blanket rule that encmpion always makes data nonsensitive.
</details>

那么，我们如何知道审查工具是否真的表现良好呢？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So how do we know whether the reviewer is actually doing a good job?
</details>

我们关注两类错误：它批准的有问题的 PR，以及它不必要地发送给人工的没问题的 PR。这一矩阵划分出了四种可能性。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
We look at two kinds of mistakes. Bad PRs it approved and fine PRs it unnecessarily send to a person. That matrix separates four possibilities.
</details>

没问题的 PR 获批，以及有问题的 PR 被呈报，是两种正确的结果。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
A fine PR approved and a bad PR escalated are the two correct outcomes.
</details>

有问题的 PR 获批属于漏报。这很危险，因为变更上线了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
A bad PR approved is a miss. That's dangerous because the change ships.
</details>

没问题的 PR 被呈报则属于误呈报，这会耗费人工时间。一个将所有内容都呈报的门禁可能会避免漏报，但并没有减少审查负荷。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
A fine PR escalated is a false escalation that costs a person's time. A gate that escalates everything may avoid misses, but it has not reduced the review load.
</details>

截至 2026 年 8 月 31 日，我们的漏报率大约是 2%。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
As of August 31st, 2026, our miss rate was about 2%.
</details>

大部分源于规则中的遗漏，而不是判断力的失误。我们没有单一明确的误呈报数据。但最主要的原因是缺少上下文，就像我刚才给你们举的加密列例子那样。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Mostly from the gaps in the rules rather than the failures of the judgment. We don't have a clean single false escalation number. But the dominant cause is missing context like the encrypted column example that I just gave you.
</details>

漏报让我们获得新规则，而误呈报向我们展示审查工具所需的上下文。这里就是其中一个漏报案例。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Misses give us new rules and false escalation show us context the reviewer needs. So here is one of those misses.
</details>

再次请注意标签。这是一个单独的例子，不是我们的模式变更 PR。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Again know the label. This is a separate example, not our schema change PR.
</details>

我们有一份某部分数据的公开副本。其中的某些列即将被重新打标，并且有一个下游读取端从该公开副本中读取这些列。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
We have an open copy of the sum of some data. some of its columns are about to be retagged and there is a downstream reader that reads those columns from the open copy.
</details>

隐私团队将这些列重新标记为个人信息。一旦发生这种情况，它们就会移入数据的一份更受限制的副本中，并从公开副本中消失。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Privacy retag those columns as personal information. When that happens, they move into a more restricted copy of the data and they are gone from the open copy.
</details>

相关的 PR 看起来没问题，而且合并了。但没有人检查的是，下游是否有任何服务仍从原先的公开副本中读取这些列。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The red PR looked fine and it merged. But what nobody checked was whether anything downstream was still reading those columns from the old open copy.
</details>

确实有服务在读。一旦这些列消失，那个读取端就崩溃了。这就是一次漏报。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
something was. And once the columns were gone, that reader broke. That's a miss.
</details>

一个本该呈报的 PR 被批准了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
A PR that should have been escalated got approved.
</details>

这不是判断力的失误，而是规则中的漏洞。这是一个先前无人写下的边缘情况。于是，它变成了一条新规则：当一列被重新标记为敏感信息时，在代码库中搜索是否有任何人仍在从即将失去该列的副本中读取它。规则就是这样变得更完善的。漏报被发现了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
It wasn't a failure of judgment. It was a gap in the rules. A corner case nobody had written down. So, it became a new rule. When a column gets retagged as sensitive, search the code base for anyone still reading it from the copy that's about to lose it. And that's how the rules get better. The misses get found.
</details>

### Jared 介绍 Bun 与 AOT 编译器

大家好。我叫 Jared。我是 Bun 的创建者。我在 Anthropic 工作。Bun 是为 Claude Code 提供支持的 JavaScript 运行时。最近我合并了一个包含数百万行代码的 PR，为 Bun 添加了一个预先编译器（AOT compiler）。基本上，其核心思想是不在运行时承担 JIT 的开销，而是可以提前把所有代码都编译好。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Hello everyone. My name is Jared. Uh I'm the creator of Bun. Uh and I work at Enthropic. Um uh Bun is the JavaScript runtime that powers cloud code. And recently I merged a several million line of code PR um to add an ahead of time compiler uh to to bond which is uh basically the idea is uh instead of paying the cost of a JIT at runtime you can uh uh you can compile all the code ahead of time. Um
</details>

这是一个非常庞大的项目。在夏天的时候，我用 Rust 重写了 Bun。而这是一个规模更大的项目。这个预先编译器比那个项目规模还要大。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
and it's it's like a really big project. Um over the summer I uh rewrote bun and rust. Um uh and this is a project on a larger scale. This ahead of time compiler is like a larger project than that. Um and
</details>

我认为目前大型项目面临的挑战之一是：你究竟如何才能放心地发布由智能体编写的数百万行代码？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
I think one of the challenges right now with large projects is like how do you actually confidently ship a mill like millions of lines of code written by agents. Um
</details>

这就是我打算要讲的内容。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
so that's what I'm going to talk about.
</details>

在进行了一些讨论之后，最初的提示词大致是：我们基本上需要让 Claude Code 减少大量内存占用并变得更快，我们可以修改 WebKit 或 Bun。Bun 底层使用的是 JavaScriptCore，也就是 WebKit。到现在已经过去一个多月了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
This was like the opening prompt after some discussion with it was like basically uh we need to make cloud code use a lot less memory and uh and faster and we can modify either webkit or bun. Um bun uses JavaScript core which is webkit um under the hood uh and it's been a little bit over a month. Um uh
</details>

这个编译器已经历了五次重写。你可以看到前几次重写都相当简短，然后我差点就把蓝色那个版本发布了。接着我觉得这构建时间实在太长了，编译代码花的时间太长了。所以又重写了一次。接下来的这一个版本将在下周发布。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
uh this is this compiler has gone through five rewrites. Um you can see the like the first couple ones were like pretty short and then I like almost shipped the blue one. Uh and then I was like this is building way too long. Like it's taking way too long to compile the code. So rewrote it again. Uh and this next one is shipping next week. Uh
</details>

我对类似这样项目的思考方式大致是：你真正受制于你如何测试代码能正常工作。所以这甚至比你具体……比如你选用哪种方式去构建它更重要，因为这完全取决于你如何验证它确实能正常工作、没有 bug、并且你是可以信赖它的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
this is kind of the way I think about a project like this is like you're really constrained by how you test that the code works. Um so that's like even more important than what you're specifically like like the approach you go with for how to build it is entirely driven by how do you verify that it actually is going to like work and not be buggy and that you can trust it.
</details>

再补充一点关于为什么做这个特定项目、为什么做预先编译器的背景：这是因为对于典型的基于 JavaScript 的应用程序，大致有一半的 CPU 时间都只是花在了按需编译代码上。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um and so just a little more background on like like why this particular project why the ahead of time compiler and it's because uh for a typical JavaScript based application roughly half of like this the CPU time is just spent compiling code on demand.
</details>

因此，大家在许多应用程序中见到的普遍现象是，人们用像 Rust 或有时用 Go 这样的语言来重写他们的代码库。从 JavaScript 转过去的时候，原因在于这确实是 JIT 加上单线程应用程序特性的共同影响。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um and so a lot of the general like thing you you've seen in a lot of applications where people are rewriting their code bases in in a language like Rust or sometimes Go um it's like when coming from JavaScript it's because uh like there it's really the the combination of the JIT and that it's a single threaded application.
</details>

这就是 JIT 和 AOT 之间的大致区别：AOT 是提前编译，而 JIT 是边运行边编译。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um so that's sort of the difference between a JIT and AOT is that uh you compile before whereas with a JIT you compile as you go along.
</details>

这对于像 Claude Code 这样的项目尤为重要，比如使用 Claude Code 的 iOS 应用时，CPU 时间非常……如果你让 Claude Code 的每一次会话也都在进行编译的话。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
This is particularly important for something like cloud code where when using like the cloud code like iOS app where the CPU time is a lot uh if you're if you have to have every session of cloud code also compiling
</details>

所以我们深入讨论过很多次：我们是否应该用 Rust 重写 Claude Code？因为 Bun 团队——也就是我们——用 Rust 重写了 Bun，所以我们在重写方面相当在行。而我们决定不这样做。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
um so we talked a lot about like like should we rewrite cloud code in rust um because the bun team us rewrote bun in rust So like we're pretty good at rewrites. Um uh and we decided not to.
</details>

原因在于，对于 Bun 而言，进行 Rust 重写其实相对容易，Bun 是一个容易进行 Rust 重写的项目，因为测试是用一种独立于其运行环境的语言编写的。由于所有测试都是用 TypeScript 编写的，我们可以在 Zig 版本和 Rust 版本上都运行这些测试；而对于 Claude Code 来说，Claude Code 的测试是用 TypeScript 编写的。因此我们必须移植 Claude Code 的测试套件，而一旦你开始重写测试，你就无法真正再信任这些测试了。所以那看起来风险太高了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um and the the reason why is because like for in bun's case it's actually like pretty easy to do like bun is an easy project to do a rust rewrite because the tests were written in a language that's independent of the one it runs in. But so because all the tests were written in Typescript um we could run the tests on both the zig version and the rust version whereas for cloud code uh we like cloud codes tests are written in typescript. So we would have to port cloud codes test suite and as soon as you're like rewriting the tests then you can't really trust the tests anymore. Um so that seemed like it'd be too risky.
</details>

所以退一步讲，要是我们直接让 JavaScript 变得更快，从而不必用 Rust 重写它呢？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So instead, what if we just make JavaScript faster so we don't have to rewrite it in Rust?
</details>

这是稍后一点的另一个提示词。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um, this was another prompt a little bit later. Um, uh,
</details>

结果是它确实把 JavaScript 的内存——比如内存占用——减少到了大约四分之一。它的启动速度大约快了 2 倍。我们正在内部进行内部试用（dogfooding），很快就会对外开放。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>

而且在提前编译器（AOT compiler）上我们有一个破坏性变更，或者实际上是两个破坏性变更，这基本上就是为什么这些特定测试会失败的原因。不过这两个破坏性变更实际上主要是一些安全方面的东西，用来防止原型污染攻击（prototype pollution attacks），但它们最终也对性能有好处。

</details>

所以，让这种特定类型的问题非常适合智能体（agents）的原因在于，你可以同时运行一个应用程序的全部三个版本：一个带 JIT，一个带提前编译，还有一个不带类型。只要这三个版本的行为一致，那你至少就知道它在这个特定应用程序上是可以正常工作的。但随后你可以把这种问题拿去进行大量模糊测试（fuzz），让 Claude 大量生成代码生成器去对不同模式进行模糊测试，通过这种方式你可以捕获大量的 bug。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
so like what makes this particular kind of problem pretty good for agents is like uh you run it with you can run all three versions of an application. One with a JIT, one with ahead of time compiled and one also without the types and as long as the three of them uh behave the same then you know that it works at least for that specific application. But then you can take that kind of problem and fuzz it a lot. Um uh and just have Claude like turn out code generators to like fuzz different patterns. Um and you can catch a lot of bugs that way. Um
</details>

### 编译器重写历程与智能体工程组织

这是一个非常庞大的项目。从代码量来看，我认为这是我做过的最大的项目。其中大部分是测试，而且也因为我们把它重写了五次。

uh this is a very large project. This is the biggest I think I've worked on. Uh in terms of like code volume, um most of this is tests. Um and also because we rewrote it five times. Um
</details>

在做这种项目的时候，我的思考方式是：你会有一批批的阶段，比如“好，我们现在需要一次性写很多代码”；而在那最初的一批之后，就会变成“好，让我们修复所有问题，确保能编译通过，确保通过大量的测试，并确保它能真正切实地工作”。具体的组织运作方式是，当你处于大批量编写的项目阶段时，你不想频繁运行构建，你只想运行一次构建，因为否则你就会把所有时间都花在编译和等待构建错误上，这不会是让任何人有效利用时间的方式。另外，在更长期的运行中，一旦你把所有代码都写完了，你就会希望拥有许多独立的智能体。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
when you're like doing this kind of project, what how I think about it is like you have like batches of like okay we need to write a lot of code at once and then after that initial batch then it's okay let's have let's like fix everything make sure it compiles make sure it passes a bunch of tests um and make sure it like really truly works. Um, and the actual logistics of that are like when you're in the big batch kind of project stage, then uh you don't want to run builds, you want to run one build because otherwise you're going to spend all your time compiling and like waiting for build errors and it's going to like not be a good use of anyone's time. Uh and then separately uh for like longer running like once you have once you've written all the code uh you want to uh have it like have lots of individual agents. Um
</details>

</details>

我以前的方法，特别是在用 Rust 重写 Bun 时，非常依赖 Claude Code 的工作流来做事。而现在换成最新的模型后，我基本上主要依靠长生命周期的子智能体（subagents）。除了最开始那个一次性把所有代码都写出来的那一部分。对于子智能体来说，这样运作得相当好，因为它们能把相关代码的所有上下文都记在脑子里，或者说保留在上下文窗口里。那样浪费更少，而且它们可以一直推进下去。

Um, and this with the sub agents, it works pretty well because they keep all the context of the relevant code in their head or like in the context window. Um, uh, and uh, that's like less wasteful. Um, and like they can just keep going. Um,
</details>

<!-- chunk 33/52 -->

大量使用解释器。因此，如果有大量类似 `eval` 或 `new Function` 的操作，那么它就不会更快。不过大多数应用程序并不会这么做。这就像……这就像是尽可能接近一行代码就能让应用程序运行得更快的做法。这并不是 100% 成立的，因为如你所见，有 UglifyJS 以及其他情况存在。但通常情况下确实是成立的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>use the interpreter a lot. So if there's a lot of like eval or new function um then it's not faster. Um but most applications don't do that. Uh and it's like it's like a make it like it's as close as possible as you can get to like a oneliner to like make an application run faster. Um this is not 100% true because as you can see uglify js and there are other cases as well. Um but it is often true.</details>

另外一点就是 `Object.prototype`。这属于罕见的极端情况，但基本上 JavaScript 应用程序中存在很多被称为原型污染（prototype pollution）的安全漏洞。这通常是通过向 `Object.prototype` 赋值属性来实现的。而编译器禁止了这种做法。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So like yeah the other thing is object prototype. Uh this is like a rare edge case but basically there's a lot of security vulnerabilities in JavaScript applications called like prototype pollution. And this usually works by assigning properties onto object.prototype. Um, and the compiler bans that.</details>

Um uh and also it tends to not be uh it tends to like underestimate what it can do. Um so I often end up saying things like this to Claude to to get it to like uh try harder.</details>

结果就是在 JavaScript 引擎主要使用的基准测试之一 JetStream 3 中，Bun 在其中绝大多数测试项上的表现都超越了 JavaScriptCore 的 JIT 以及 Node.js（即 V8 JIT）。而且整个过程中，一直都在使用 Claude Code 来构建 `bun compile` 本身，然后使用通过 `bun compile` 编译构建出的 Claude Code。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>And the result is that uh Jetream 3, which is one of the main benchmarks that JavaScript engines use, um uh the bun outperforms both JavaScript cores JIT and no Node.js in like V8 JIT um in most of them. Uh, and the whole time like it's been using cloud code to like build the bun compile like to build bun compile itself and then using the build of cloud code that was compiled with bun compile.</details>

我对这件事情的看法大概是这样：在与 Claude 合作开展大型项目时，可测试性这件事非常重要，必须确保测试确实测到了东西。如果你不积极地重新测量，情况就会变差，因为涉及的内容太多，很容易跟丢。对于这类尚未发布的大型项目，重写往往比重构更容易。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Um uh so sort of how I think about this is like like the testability thing is super important when you're working on a large project with claude. Uh and to make sure that the tests actually test things. Um and if you're not actively reme-measuring then it's going to like get worse because it's just if there's so much stuff going on it's really easy to lose track. Um uh and like it's easier for large projects like this that haven't shipped yet to uh rewrite than it is to refactor.</details>

如果你不抱有雄心去行动，模型带来的改进就会开始变成 0.1%，而不是 10%。所以你必须不断督促它：“嘿，只要你更加努力去尝试，就能做出非常酷的事情。”如果你安装了 Bun，现在就可以尝试一下，运行 `bun upgrade --canary`，然后直接运行 `bun compile` 即可。谢谢大家。

>> 好的。这是本次演讲的提纲。我们将探讨 Uber 目前的代码合并是如何运作的，接着介绍 AI 如何改变了这一格局，随后介绍我们的应对举措以及如何进行适应，然后讨论各项成果，并聊聊我们接下来的规划。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>>> Great. Um so here's the outline for the talk. Uh we're going to go over like how the merging um works at Uber at the moment and then how AI changed the landscape and then I'll respond um to that how we adapted and then discuss the results and um talk about what are we planning next.</details>

### Uber 的单体仓库与合并队列

在 Uber，我们有 6 个基于语言的 monorepo，超过 5,000 名开发者每周贡献超过 36,000 次 commit 和 27,000 个 PR，并且对服务和库的绝大部分更改都是在这些 monorepo 之一中完成的。在本次演讲中，我们在讨论任何内容时都会主要聚焦在 Go 代码上，因为那是我们最大的一个代码库，也是我们面临绝大多数扩展性挑战的地方。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So at Uber uh we have a six language based mono repos where more than 5,000 uh developers uh are contributing over 36,000 commits uh 27,000 PRs uh every week and uh pretty much every changes uh to the services libraries are being done in one of these monor repos. uh for the purpose of this talk we're going to focus mostly on go code when we talk about anything because that is our the biggest one and that's where the most of the scaling challenges uh we face.</details>

我们来理解一下这个问题，弄清楚为什么我们这里需要一个合并队列。这里基本上是有两位用户，用户 A 和用户 B，在主干的一个 base 边缘上分别创建了 PR A 和 PR B。当创建 PR 并运行 CI 时，CI 是绿色的，构建通过，一切正常。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So let's understand the problem a little bit and um and understand like why we need a merge cube here. So uh given here basically we have two users uh user A and B creating PR A and B um on a base edge uh from the trunk and when the PRs are created and CI is run uh the CI is green bills are passing everything is good.</details>

现在，当 A 被合并到主干中时，我们可以看到合并 commit 的构建仍然是绿色的，构建依然通过；但当 PR B 被合并到主干中时，构建不再是绿色的了。原因是 B 从未与 A 合并的更改一起进行过测试。我们在合并前从未测试过这两者的组合，因此当 B 合并时就坏掉了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Now uh when a uh is merged into the trunk uh we can see the bills for the merge commit are still green u bills are still passing but when the PRB is merged into the trunk um bills are no longer green and the reason is that B was never tested uh with the changes that were merged by A. So uh we never tested the the combination of those two things before merging and hence the the when B is merged it's broken.</details>

现在设想一下，当有数千名工程师在同一个 monorepo 中工作，每个人每天都在进行数百次乃至数千次这样的更改。这就成了一个持续存在的问题：主干非常频繁地损坏，造成了修复的负担，并在全公司范围内带来了排查和修复的损耗。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Um now imagine when we have thousands of uh engineers working in a monor repo and everyone is making changes u like hundreds and thousands of those every day. Uh this is a constant problem uh where trunk is broken um very frequently and there is a burden on fixing and there is a toll across the company to sort of um figure it out and uh fix it.</details>

针对这个问题，我们在这里介绍一种解决方案，那就是 Summit 队列。这是我们部署用来解决此问题的 Uber 合并队列。在这种情况下，我们可以看看两个示例 PR，PR A 和 PR B。这里我们实质上在做的是预测在 main 分支上可能成立的未来状态。这里存在三种情况：A 单独合入 main 分支，B 单独合入 main 分支，以及 A 和 B 的组合。总的来说，我认为我们想要做的是合入能够让主干整体保持绿色的更改。在这个例子中，当我们把 A 和 B 一起合入时，主干是绿色的，这就是 Summit 队列的大致运作方式，从而让我们的 monorepo 保持主干绿色。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Um so here uh we're going to present a solution uh to that problem uh and that solution is summit Q. Um that is our Uber's merge Q um that we deploy uh to solve this problem. And in this case we can see our two example CRP A and PRB. And here we essentially what we are doing is we are predicting the futures that would be uh that could be true on main. So there are like three things here. A could land on on its own to the main B could land on its own to the main and then the combination of A and B. In general I think uh what we want to do is that we want to land the change that is going to keep the trunk green as a whole. So in this case when we land A and B together the trunk is green and uh and that's how basically uh sort of summit Q works and keeps the trunk green for our monor repos.</details>

那么在这一版本中它是如何扩展的呢？Summit 采用了三项机制。第一项是目标分析（target analysis）。这意味着当两位用户在两个不同的服务中工作时，他们的更改并没有任何重叠。因此这两部分更改可以相互独立地进行构建和测试，然后再进行合并。这使我们基本上能够获得更高的吞吐量，而无需对进入队列的每一个更改都进行串行化处理。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Now how does it scale in this uh version of it? Um there are three things that summit employs. U the very first thing is target analysis. Uh what it means is when two users are working across two different services uh they don't really have any overlapping changes. Um so those two things can be built and tested independently of each other and then can be merged. So this allows us to basically get higher throughput and not serialize every change that is entering into the queue.</details>

第二项是概率推测（probabilistic speculation）。它的含义是，我们利用历史构建信息以及更改的特征，来预测给定更改的成功分数。然后我们借此来决定为了让该更改合入，可能需要哪些构建。因此在这种情况下，相比于推测产生的所有其他构建，我们优先考虑那些最可能需要、且对该更改合并到主干中至关重要的构建。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Uh second is probabilistic speculation. Um what it means is uh we are using historical um the build information and also the the change characteristics to predict uh what is the success uh score for a given change. And then we are using that to basically decide what are bills are likely needed in order to land that change. And so in that case we prioritize the build that are most likely needed and are important for that change to be uh merged to the trunk rather than like every other build that uh comes from the speculation.</details>

第三部分是旁路大变更（bypassing the large changes）。它的意思是，如果队列中有一个正在耗费较长时间构建的庞大变更链，而与此同时有较小的更改进入队列，这些较小的更改可以更快构建完成，因此它们无需等待大变更合入之后才能合入。这样一来，我们可以在不预先合入较大更改的情况下合入较小的更改，这项优化使我们基本上能够快速推进并获得更高的队列吞吐量。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>The third part is bypassing the large changes. Uh what it means is that if we have a large chain sitting in the queue which is taking longer to build and at the same time if we have a smaller changes entering into the queue um those can be built faster and so though those changes don't need to wait for large changes to land before they can land. So in this case we can land the smaller changes without landing the larger changes first and this optimization allows us to basically move fast and get a higher throughput of the queue.</details>

在只有人类合入更改、创建 PR 时，这套机制一直运作良好。但随后我们迎来了 AI，PR 创建和合入的速度全然改变了。为了更好地理解这一点，我们来看一些数据。在 2026 年 1 月，我们大约每小时处理 90 个请求，而到了 3 月，我们每小时处理 130 个请求。也就是说在两个月内大约增长了 44%，而这一增长主要归因于 AI 生成的更改。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>So this is what has worked um when it was just humans uh landing changes creating PRs but um then we had AI and the changes at which the uh PRs were created and landed it all changed. So to understand it a better let's look at some of the numbers. So uh in January 2026 we were doing uh roughly 90 requests uh every hour and by March we were doing 130 requests every hour. So that's roughly a 44% increase in two months and the reason for this increase is primarily because of the AI generated changes.</details>

鉴于系统的构建方式，这些更改的速率对其产生了影响。来看看这些情况：当我们每小时处理 90 个请求时，P95 排队时间为 30 分钟；而当我们每小时处理 130 个请求时，排队时间大约增加到了 3 到 4 个小时。在这种情况下，我们虽然始终在保持主干处于绿色状态，但是……

<details><summary>Original English</summary>Now given the way the system was built um uh it had the rate of uh these changes had effect on it. So um looking into those like we had 30 minutes of uh P95 line times when we were doing 90 requests every hour and that went to roughly 3 to four hours when we were doing uh 130 uh requests every hour. So in this case we were still keeping the trunk green uh at all times but</details>

我们来看看准入关控的权衡。在实施这一点后，我们确实观察到了吞吐量的增长。我们实现了超过 20% 的增长。在工作时间内，我们的合入率（land rate）从 130 左右上升到了约 160。但这是有代价的，代价就是开发人员变成了调度器。并不是所有开发人员都这样，因为我们确实保护了较小的改动。但你可以想象，如果你是一名拥有横跨整个代码的任意类型代码库的开发人员，你在这当中会受到特别的针对，因为你所做的工作天然就落入了这个高开销改动的分类中。所以我们在高峰时段保护了容量，但不得不让开发人员等待。下一张幻灯片。

### 推迟队列与冲突放宽

所以我们做的下一件事，更多是为了改善准入关控带来的弊端中的开发人员体验。要是我们不去直接拒绝高开销改动，而是能够将它们记录下来，并根据队列的状态将它们重放回主队列，会怎样呢？比如在我们对容量有更好的掌控时。而这正是推迟机制所实现的。在高峰时段，如果有高开销请求进来，我们不再拒绝它，而是将其放入推迟队列中进行持久化保存。

### 人类判断与软件工程的核心

我想讲的最后一个原则，可以说是适用于所有这些内容的。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The last principle I want to go over kind of applies to all of this.
</details>

本次大会上有很多关于软件工厂的精彩演讲。大家知道，这确实非常令人兴奋。我们可以为我们的智能体提供构建更多内容、更可靠地进行验证以及更快发布的方法。这很令人振奋。但是，如果以往软件工程的所有构成部分都被抽象化了，那么我们该如何定义人类判断所剩余的价值呢？我认为人类判断比以往任何时候都更加重要。如果我让一个智能体去“把这张幻灯片做好一点”，也许它会添加一些可视化图表、一堆仪表盘、炫酷的色彩等等。也许它会用 Rust 重写整个幻灯片展示程序。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
There's a lot of great talks at this conference about software factories. You know, it's really exciting. We can give our agents ways to build more, verify more reliably, and ship faster. It's exciting. But if all the parts of what software engineering used to look like get abstracted away, then how do we define what the remaining value in our human judgment is? I'll argue that human judgment is more important than ever. If I ask an agent to say, make this slide better, maybe it'll add some visualizations and a bunch of dashboards and cool colors and everything. Maybe it'll rewrite the whole slideshow in Rust.
</details>

所以，它会解决每一个问题。但每一个问题都需要被解决吗？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So, it'll solve every problem. But does every problem need to be solved?
</details>

以大约一年前 Anti-gravity 刚推出时智能体管理器的样子为例。如果我们想在智能体看来让它变得更好，那可能意味着添加更多细节、发布所有东西、在智能体采取的每一个步骤中加入更多细节，并为用户提供控制一切的旋钮。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Take for instance what the agent manager looked like around a year ago when anti-gravity first launched if we want to make it better to an agent. That might look like adding a lot more detail, ship everything, add more detail into every single step the agent is taking and give the user knobs for everything
</details>

这在某些方面可能更好。但这是盲目的。我们很容易盲目接受摆在我们面前的第一个方案。对我们而言，“更好”究竟意味着什么？如果我们试图打造一个更好的智能体管理器界面，也许那看起来会有所不同。也许我们需要快速发布，但在迭代过程的每一步都要理解对我们而言“更好”究竟意味着什么，以及下一个方向是什么。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
might be better in some ways. But it's blind. It's easy to blindly accept the first thing that we're given. What does better even mean to us? If we're trying to make a better agent manager interface, maybe that looks a little bit different. Maybe we need to ship fast, but understand at each step of the iteration process what better actually means to us and what the next direction is.
</details>

让我们从这里开始。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Let's start here.
</details>

更好可能意味着简化界面，在语义上只向用户准确展示智能体当前正在做的最新事情，这样他们就不会面对满屏可能根本无关紧要的文字和工具调用。也许它看起来像是简化整个界面以减轻用户的认知负担，从而使他们能够更轻松地进行多任务处理，并能够以不同的方式实现更多目标。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Better might mean simplifying the interface, only showing the user exactly what the latest thing the agent is doing semantically so they're not shown a wall of text and tool calls that might not actually matter. Maybe it looks like simplifying the whole interface to give the user less cognitive load so that they can multitask easier and be able to achieve more in a different way.
</details>

因此，这让我们更接近 Anti-gravity 今天的样子。这一过程中的所有这些步骤，都让我们更接近“优秀”的含义。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So this kind of brings us closer to what anti-gravity looks like today. All these steps along the process have brought us closer to what good means.
</details>

而且我们正在快速进行大量改进。因此，我非常期待大家能看到它。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And we're making a lot of improvements fast. So I'm excited you all for you all to see it.
</details>

现在正是尝试多种事物的时候。要有雄心。现在比以往任何时候都更令人振奋。而且随着模型变得越来越好，实际上还会变得更加令人兴奋。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now is the time to try many things. Be ambitious. It's more exciting than ever. And it'll actually get more exciting as the models get better.
</details>

尝试许多方向，但花点时间思考。运用你的人类判断力。仅仅因为你率先完成了某件事的创建（这在瞬间就能完成），并不意味着你真正完成了。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Try many directions, but take a moment to think. Use your human judgment. Just because you're finished creating something first, which can be done in an instant, it doesn't actually mean you're done.
</details>

思考一下你真正想要实现的是什么，以及你正在制作的东西给人什么样的感受。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Think about what you're actually trying to achieve and how the thing you're making feels.
</details>

快速迭代。确定你可以走的许多方向并尝试它们，但要找到正确的事物并深入钻研进去。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Iterate quickly. Identify many directions you can go in and try them, but arrive at the right thing and go deep into that.
</details>

因此，要留出思考的时间，留出思考的空间，并运用你的人类判断力。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So make time to think, make room to think, and use your human judgment.
</details>

总结一下，我们学到的一些经验包括：随着智能的扩展，这并不总是意味着添加更多内容。它可能意味着重塑你的基础。依托这些基础，让你的用户以你之前可能未曾预料到的新方式去怀抱雄心。在所有这一切的最后，当我们发布得越来越快时，留出思考的空间，守护软件工程中属于人性的那一面。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
To summarize, some of the lessons we've learned involve if scaling up with intelligence, which doesn't always mean adding more. It could mean rebuilding your foundations. Take these foundations and enable your users to be ambitious in new ways you might not have expected before. And at the end of all this, when we're shipping faster and faster, make room to think and protect the human side of software engineering.
</details>

在 Google，通过打造一个承载雄心的平台，这带来了一些非常棒的成果：对 YouTube 的改进、更好的视频解码器以及内存优化。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>

### 区分新建探索与既有维护

因此，这当然也就是我该呼吁大家请停止构建软件工厂的时候了，对吧？呃，所以我——我显然有点在开玩笑，但也同样是认真的。呃，我认为你们真的应该停止将自己的构想、雄心和目标强加于工厂流程之上。因为大多数时候我们并不清楚自己想要什么；或者说至少很多时候我们在创造新事物时，我们尚不知道自己想要什么。如果我们试图去把一个连自己想要什么都不知道的东西工业化，我们肯定无法得到正确的结果，或者会耗费太长时间，亦或投入过高成本。它会在多个层面令人感到遗憾。

### 将承诺编码为闭环

关于如何思考棕地项目的处理方式、清理它并使其具备自给自足能力的几点说明：在 Diffusion，我们使用“承诺”（promise）这个词，来代表我们在产品中所看重的某项保证、某个方面、某个优先级和某个维度。功能是一项承诺，东西的成本与定价是一项承诺，安全性也是一项承诺。而且你极少会想要违背或倒退自己的承诺。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
and a couple of uh a couple of notes about just ways of thinking about working with your brown field and you know cleaning it up and making it self-sustaining. Um uh at diffusion we use the word promise um to represent you know some guarantee some aspect some priority and some dimension within the product that we value. Okay. feature is a promise. Um, you know, the cost and price of a thing is a promise. Um, the security of a thing is a promise. Um, and it's rare that you want to revert and regress on your promises. Okay.
</details>

所以，如果你能够开始讨论我们对既有产品所抱有的各项预期，你就可以开始将它们编码进回路之中。无论你用 YAML 文件、某种 Python 代码还是图来表示，回路就像我们一直在讨论的任何工厂方案一样，它具备某种特征：接收输入，产生输出，然后再以某种方式反馈回去。正如你们许多人所知道的，这是放大器的理想化框图；如果切断那条反馈回路，你最终就只会放大噪声。我认为大家现在都有这种体会，尤其是我们一直在与软件工厂及反馈回路打交道。必须有某种方法将这种保真度反馈到系统的输入端。但如果你做到了并且调校得当，它就能良好运转。

这很有意思，因为通常情况下，当你只是让智能体写代码时，你处于自己的会话中，让它写一个 PR，它往往只是读取你的会话日志，利用其膨胀的上下文窗口试图弄明白自己原本应该做什么。实际上，随着会话轮次的推进，它经常会压缩自己的上下文窗口。所以随着过程的深入，它会变得越来越不准确。因此，我们在这里通过 trace tune 所做的，是让它去通读你电脑上的各个不同会话，真正弄清楚你构建了什么以及到底发生了什么。

It actually often like as it's turnurning it compresses its context window. So it gets more and more inaccurate as it goes along. So instead, what we're doing here is with trace tune, we're having it read through uh different sessions on your computer to try to really figure out what you built and what happened.
</details>

### 嵌入式图数据库 Archie

>> 是的。是的。总会有一个新的图。但这并不是同一个老图。这不是 Neo Forj 数据库。这是我们的新朋友 Archie，它是一个用 Rust 编写的嵌入式数据库，名叫 relate，实际上这周正在 NODES 大会以及旧金山 AI Engineer 的这场演讲上发布。它是一个开源数据库，能让我们在无需采用重量级方案的情况下，获得更高准确率、多跳查询以及更具解释性的跟踪记录等诸多好处。所以它有点像是一个专门针对这类嵌入式使用场景、面向 Agent 的图数据库。
<details><summary>Original English</summary>
>> Yeah. Yeah. There's always a new graph. But this is not the same old graph. This is not the the Neo Forj database. This is our new friend Archie who is a embedded database written in Rust called relate that actually is being launched this week at the nodes conference and also this talk at a engineer in San Francisco and it's an open-source um database which allows us to get a lot of the benefits of more accuracy multihop queries more explainable traces is without having the heavyweight approach. So it's kind of an agent focused graph database specifically for these sort of embedded use cases.
</details>

好，那我们来看看 Archie 能怎么帮我们。之前我们很大程度上依赖 Agent 以及 Agent 循环来解决问题。但图数据库可以存储关系，可以找出聚类在一起的不同事物，构建一份将所有这些信息连接在一起的摘要，然后为 Agent——哪怕是更便宜的 Agent——提供更好的信息和更多的上下文，从而做出良好的起草决策。Archie 的好处在于他完全没有 LLM 成本。全都是本地计算和存储成本，而且它是一个完全内存式的数据库，用 Rust 编写，所以高度优化。因此，与我们之前承担的所有 LLM 和 token 成本相比，我们基本上是免费获得了关于所有这些提交更改的出处信息以及这个证据图。这意味着我们的伙伴 Alex 不需要再去调研了。他不需要再去梳理并重新找出所有事实、查遍所有来源，因为 relate 会替他完成所有这些繁重的工作，并构建一个可用于 TraceTune 的证据包。
<details><summary>Original English</summary>

And the good thing about Archie is he has zero LLM costs. It's all local compute cost and storage and it's a fully immemory database and rest so it's highly optimized. And this means our our buddy Alex doesn't need to research. She doesn't need to go through and find all the facts again and go through the sources because relate is going to do all that hard work for him and build an evidence packet that he can use for trace tune.
</details>

### 利用 KV Cache 打造智能体安全防线

>> 大家好，我是 Pinter AI 的创始人 Arius。呃，这场演讲是面向那些正在构建智能体并且运行着自己的推理技术栈，或者正打算这么做的人。如果你正是如此，你通常会将 KV Cache——现代推理引擎的立身之本——仅仅看作一种吞吐量优化手段。每个 Token 计算得到的 Key 和 Value 被保留下来，这样模型就不用重复计算它们了。今天，我想从一个不同的视角来展示 KV Cache。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
>> Hi, I'm Arius, founder of Pinter AI. Um, this talk is for people who build agents and run their own inference stack or plan to. And if that's you, you associate typically the KB cache, the bread and butter of modern inference engines usually as a throughput optimization. The keys and values computed per token kept so the model doesn't compute them again. Today I like to show KD cache in a different light.
</details>

AI 智能体应用出状况的方式越来越多，对吧？提示词注入、越狱、破损的工具调用、敏感工具调用、传感器数据泄露、隐藏后门以及漂移等等，对吧？所以目前我们防御这些可能导致 AI 智能体应用出错的诸多途径时，通常的做法是在边缘进行防护，比如通过引入一个裁判模型（judge model），根据你的策略来检查提示词输入和响应输出。对吧？或者你针对每种情况分别构建一个分类器。但这里提供了一个不同的视角：模型在行动之前、在解码之前、在生成响应之前、在该响应变成另一次工具调用之前，都会将每一个输入转化为计算。KV Cache 就是你的引擎对那次计算的记录，而现代推理引擎免费地使用它并读取它。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
The way agents agent AI agent application could go wrong are keep growing, right? Prom injection, jailbreaks, broken tool calls, sensitive tool calls, sensor data exposures, hidden back doors and drips and so forth, right? So the way right now we typically guard against against these numerous ways that an AI agent application could go wrong is by guarding the edge by perhaps passing a judge model that checks the prompts the inputs and the response outputs against your policy. All right? Or you build a classifier for each case. But here is a different light. The model turns every input into a computation before it acts before it decodes before it generates the response before that response becomes another tool call. The KV cache is your engine's record of that computation that modern inference engine for free are using it and reading it.
</details>

因此在本次演讲中，我想建议的是，作为对你们目前使用自身安全护栏（guardrails）方式的补充，利用这些数据并将其与你们的 KV Cache 基础设施集成起来。我把这称为“KV 优先的安全信号”（KV-first security signals），核心在于有三个重要的理由，对吧。首先，数据早就存在了。你是从推理技术栈原本随手扔掉的废料堆里拾取珍珠。其次，它覆盖了安全护栏的盲区，因为无论什么东西溜过了那些护栏，它依然会转化为计算并留存下来。因此，它非常适用于数据隐私应用，适用于你没有权限读取输入和输出的场景。实现方法相当直截了当，对吧？它分为三个步骤：你为你的人工智能工作负载、为你的 AI 推理流量学习一个基线，了解你的 KV Page 通常是什么样子；然后找出相比于基线属于低频罕见的 KV Page，这通常就是你的异常；接着你甚至可以对它们进行分类和解释。那么首先，让我们从该集成可以在技术栈的什么位置切入开始讲起。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
And therefore in this talk I would like to suggest as as a complement to the way you're already using your own guardrails to use this data and integrated with your KB cache infrastructure. I refer to this as a KB first security signals and basically three reasons that matters right. So the data is already there. You pick the gems from the pile your inference stack already was throwing away. It covers the guardrails blind spots because whatever slips past those guardrails, it still becomes computation and it stays in place. So it's perfect for data privacy application for cases where you do not have the luxury to read the inputs and outputs. And the recipe is pretty straightforward, right? It has three steps. You learn a baseline for your AI workload, for your AI inference traffic, how your KV page typically look like and then find the KV pages that are rare against your baseline typically your anomalies and then you can even classify and explain those. So first let's start with where in this stack this integration could happen.
</details>

关于 KV Cache，有趣的一点在于，它们的存在在某种程度上让推理变成了有状态的（stateful），对吧。例如，像 LMCache 这样的 KV Cache 管理层会将它们从 GPU 转存到远程存储；或者像 LLM-D 和 Nvidia Dynamo 会将每个新请求路由到可能已经持有该缓存 KV Page 的 Worker 上。所以有两个集成点，你可以把这些异常检测或那些分类器集成进去：要么你可以直接在你的推理引擎（vLLM 等等）内部实现它。这种方式的妙处在于它是实时的，而且它可能生成的警报可以伴随你未来的工具调用或未来的策略一同传递。这样一来，仅凭在推理引擎内部观察到的计算异常，你基本上就能强化你的策略算法。

The interesting thing about the KB cache is that in a way existence of those had made the inference in a way stateful right. and perhaps you can integrate it with as a as a way of thread discovery in a sense so you can integrate with your LM cache so it reads the stored KB pages and find things that don't belong with the rest right so now I want to show you some numbers and and just a very simple hello world use case if this is actually working this is a valid approach and for that let me use with a use case that has no text to filter at all a voice agent.

这里就是一个语音智能体的案例研究。在这个案例中，使用的是 Gemma 4 E4B。我们的基线是 72 段常规音频剪辑，由四种声音讲出。我们观察的是 16-token 的 Page。显然这种粒度是可以改变的，但在这里我们只是使用了较短的 Page，以便基线中能有更多的数据。然后在测试中，我们使用了不同的声音，其中一个是纯净的声音，而另一个声音内部带有一条隐藏指令。正如这里所展示的，你不仅可以通过简单的异常检测来发现这些情况，而且你还能知道具体是哪一部分——呃，哪个 Page，你的请求的哪一部分发生了这个异常事件。我们甚至更进一步，训练了一个将 KV Page 作为特征的模型，并且我们没打算解释它或用有用的内容给它打标签。在这种情况下，尽管该模型甚至没有在音频上进行过训练，它却能够生成关于个人信息、家庭住址、位置数据、出行信息和目的地的正确信息。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
So here's a case study of a voice agent. In this case it was a GMO 4 E4B. Our baseline was 72 ordinary clips and spoken four voices. We're looking at 16 token pages. Obviously this granular granularity could change but here we just use shorter pages so we can have more data in the baseline. And then on the test we have different voices and one clean voice and the other one has a hidden instruction within it. And as you can as is shown here not only you can detect these cases just using simple anomaly detection but also you would know which part of um which page which part of your request had this anomalous event. We even went further and we trained a model that uses the KV pages as features and we didn't try to explain it or tag it with things that are useful. </details>

现在回到文本智能体。在这个案例中，是一个 Qwen 3 14B Live 在 L11 Cache 之下。左边的图向你展示了攻击流量与正常流量通常是如何相互区分的概念。而右边可能更有意义的那张图，使用的是 Hugging Face 上的 AgentBench。它拥有由良性智能体轨迹构成的自身基线，以及八种智能体故障，从注入的工具描述到错误信息等等。我们观察了异常检测会生成的 Top 10 警报，其中大约有 7 到 8 次这些警报实际上都是有意义且有用的异常活动。我们甚至更进一步，探究了是否存在任何比其余事件更为突出的异常事件类型。

So now back to text agents. In this case, it was a quan 3 14 billion live and below 11 cache. The one on the left shows you an idea for how attacks and normal traffic would typically separate from each other. And then the one on the right, which is perhaps more meaningful, it was using the at bench and hugging face. 呃，有两点让生产环境下的事情变得困难。首先是模型的变动。不仅有微调和新版本发布，还有每个运行在不同模型上的智能体，以及为每个请求挑选专长模型的路由器。这意味着每个模型都会以自己的坐标系来写入它的 Page。所以要么你去学习每一个基线、分类器和解释器，这在计算上是极其昂贵的；要么你就必须找到一种方法，将它们全部映射到同一个共享的表示空间中。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um, two make things hard in production. First, models change. fine tunes and new releases, but also agents that each run on a different model and routers that pick a specialist model per request. That means each model writes its pages its own coordinates. So either you learn every baseline, classifier, and explainer, which is computationally prohibitive, or you have to find a way to map them all into one shared representation space.
</details>

其次，实时意味着要搬运大对象。例如，Qwen 3 14B 的一个 1256-token 块大约对应 40MB 的 KV。压缩和流式传输（比如 HashGen）可能会有所帮助，但为了安全分析而搬运这些数据并在实时中采取行动依然很慢，而这正是我们在……所解决的问题。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Second, real time means moving big objects. For example, a 1256 token chunk of coin 314 billion is about 40 megabytes of KV compression and the streaming like hashgen could help, but moving that for security analysis and acting in real times is still a slow and that's what we solve at
</details>

### 后训练的工程挑战与闭环

嗯，现在我想关于模型的市场宣传已经足够了。我想，有人告诉我台下是非常懂技术的受众。因此我想稍微谈谈为什么对这些前沿模型进行后训练是困难的，以及这其中涉及哪些环节。首先我想说，大家知道，当今在谷歌，AI 编写了大约 75% 的新增代码。而这在几年前还不到 25%。并且每天在谷歌内部有 100,000 人使用 Antigravity 编写代码。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
now I think enough marketing with the models. I want to I was told this is a deeply technical audience. So I want to talk a little bit about why post- training these frontier models is hard and what goes into that. Let me start by saying, you know, AI writes about 75% of Google's new code today. And this is up from less than 25% a few years ago. And there are 100,000 people using anti-gravity writing code inside Google every single day.
</details>

如今，大家所执行的任务的复杂度在过去几年中也呈指数级增长。我们最初是从大家知道的模型单纯进行聊天补全开始的，那只需要花费几秒钟，发展到在几分钟内编写小型函数。大家可以提交 PR 并解决一个 issue，大约需要说一小时左右，但现在人们正在利用该模型执行长达数天的真正工程任务。人们拥有智能体群（swarms of agents），利用该模型解决真正的开放性问题。在整个谷歌，我们的工程师正在使用 Antigravity 和我们的 Gemini 4 模型来跨越数天解决任务。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now, the complexity of the tasks that people do have also increased exponentially in the last couple of years. We started with you know models just completing chats which took seconds to writing small functions within the minutes. You can submit a PR and resolve an issue takes about an hour let's say but now people are doing days of real engineering tasks with the model. People have swarms of agents solving real open problems with the model. We have engineers across Google using anti-gravity and our Gemini 4 models to solve tasks over multiple days.
</details>

那么，我们究竟如何对 Gemini 进行后训练以应对真正的工程任务呢？我们必须从高难度任务入手。因此，我们确实非常需要为模型搜集难题。对这些模型进行评分实际上是一个非常困难的问题。所以，你希望像一位经验丰富的代码审查员那样去打分。你希望像一位经验丰富的工程师评审解决方案那样去打分。大规模强化学习（RL）就是我们训练模型的方式。同时我们希望在拥有 100,000 名工程师的社区中对我们的模型进行内部试用（dogfood），并循环往复。这样，内部试用中的任何失败情况都会成为下一次循环迭代的任务。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now, how do we go about post- training Gemini for real engineering tasks? We have to start with hard tasks. So, we need really need to source hard problems for the model. Grading of these models is actually a very hard problem. So, you want to grade like an experienced code reviewer would grade. You want to grade like an experienced engineer would grade a solution. Large scale RL is how we train the model. And we want to dog food our models with our 100,000 engineer community and repeat the loop. So any failures in the dog food become tasks for the next iteration of the loop.
</details>

所以我想先对这些部分的每一个做更进一步的详细介绍。那么让我们从高难度任务开始，对吧？嗯，大家可能听说过，随着模型变得越来越好，许多基准测试都趋于饱和，而且不仅基准测试趋于饱和，我们的训练数据也同样趋于饱和。这里有一张图表，展示了针对 Gemini 4 模型的三个不同迭代版本，可学习提示词（learnable prompts）的份额下降了多少。比方说我们在第一次迭代中有 x% 的可学习提示词。到我们完成并训练下一个版本的时候，这些提示词就已经太简单了

So I want to start by going into a little bit more detail of each of these sections. So let's start with the hard tasks, right? Um so you might have heard you know as models are getting better lots of benchmarks are getting saturated and it's not just the benchmarks that get saturated our training data gets saturated too. So here is a graph that shows for three different iterations of the Gemini 4 models how much uh the share of learnable prompts has gone down. ### 困难任务与评分标准

……针对第二个模型。我们完成那个之后，接着训练第三个模型。对第三个模型来说，这些 prompt 太简单了。那么在简单的任务上进行训练有什么问题呢？简单的任务给你带来的学习信号是零，对吧？也就是说，你给出一个 prompt，所有的 rollout 都会是正确的。这里面没有方差，没有任何强化学习信号。所以你真的需要专注于获取困难任务，而且你获取困难任务的速度真的必须快于模型变强的速度，对吧？

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
for the second one. We finish that and train the third one. The prompts are too easy for the third one. So what's the problem with training on easy task? Easy task give you zero learning signal, right? So you have a prompt when all of your rollouts are going to be correct. There's no variance. There's no RL signal. So you really need to focus on getting hard tasks and you really need to be faster at getting hard tasks than the models are getting better, right?
</details>

嗯，那么我们该怎么做呢？我们首先得识别出这些简单的任务并修剪掉它们。因此，我们从数据混合中剔除这些对模型来说太简单的简单 band 风格任务。我们拓展到难度大得多的 suite 任务中，例如多文件代码库。我们从单元测试或二元正确性转变为基于评分标准（rubric）的评分。评分标准能给你密集的信号，它们不仅仅是 0 或 1，在评估准则内部可以有更多的梯度。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Um so how do we go about it? We first have to you know identify these easy tasks and prune them. So we cut these simple band style tasks from the mixture that are too easy for the models. We expand into much harder sweet tasks for example that are multi-file repos. We go from unit test or binary correctness um to rubric based grading. And rubrics give you dense signal. They're not just 01. there can be um more gradients within the um within the criteria.
</details>

这里有一些我们用来训练的困难任务的例子。我们研究多文件代码库，并从中提取 commit。模型会自动从这些 commit 中提取出评分标准。我们实际上并不强制要求涉及单元测试，我们完全可以基于这些代码库动态生成评分标准。另一个例子，一个绝佳的任务例子是：尝试教模型从零构建 CLI 工具。你从一个空文件夹和一份规格说明（specification）开始。规格说明上写着：嘿，你必须构建这个工具，它叫 GP，这些是 GP 的参数，这些是 flag，这是具体功能。然后你让模型从零构建出整个工具。因为你知道 GRP 的功能会是什么样，所以你拥有一个隐藏的测试套件，可以在上面对生成的二进制文件进行评估。最后一个例子是生产代码库的 diff。你会去观察模型在生产代码库内部的真实使用情况，并从中提取出有用的任务。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Here are some examples of hot tasks that we train on. So uh we look at multifile repos and we extract commits from them. From those commits we have models automatically extract rubrics. Um we don't actually ne necessitate unit tests being involved. We can actually uh generate rubrics on the fly based on these repos. Another example, a great example of tasks are you try to teach the model to build uh CLI tools from scratch. So you start with an empty folder and a specification. The specification says, hey, you have to build this tool. It's called GP. These are the parameters of GP. These are the flags. This is the functionality. And you let the model build the entire tool from scratch. And you know what the functionality for GRP is going to be. So you have a hidden test suite that you can evaluate the binary on. The last uh example is production codebased diffs. So you look at real usage of the model inside your production codebase and extract useful tasks out of them.
</details>

### 防范作弊与裁判系统加固

现在让我来稍微谈一下评分裁判（grader）。

智能体，你知道的，因为你是在训练它们通过测试，所以它们会想方设法去通过测试。但即使测试通过了，也并不一定意味着它们真正解决了问题。有些例子里，智能体会绕过检查器；有些例子里，智能体会钻 benchmark 的漏洞；还有些例子里，智能体会伪造证据。你知道的，它把伪造的证据写入一个临时文件，然后删除任何表明它伪造证据的痕迹，对吧？所以，随着这些模型变得越来越强大，你必须用更聪明的方式来评判它们。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now let me talk a little bit about uh the graders.

So agents you know they find any way you're you're training them to pass the test. So they will find any way to pass the test. But if the test pass, that doesn't necessarily mean that they've solved the problem. There are examples where the agent will bypass the checker. There are examples where the agent will game the benchmark. There are examples where the agent will fake the evidence. You know, it writes the uh fake evidence into a temp file. It'll delete any evidence of it faking it, right? Um, so you have to be smarter about how you judge these models, especially as they become more capable.
</details>

这就是我所说的“像代码审查者一样评分”。如果你做过代码审查，面对一个非常严格的审查者，你会知道他们不会只考虑方案的正确性。他们实际上还会考虑代码风格、可维护性，以及许多不同的方面。所以我们所做的，就是从基于单元测试的正确性转变为基于评分标准，而这些标准是根据真实 commit 生成的。评分标准还能提供密集的学习信号，而不仅仅是 0 或 1 的信号，并且我们会惩罚任何试图投机取巧走捷径的方案，对吧？这是一个评分标准的例子，其中的评估准则是：需要通过哪些边缘情况、需要维护哪种代码规范整洁度、需要满足哪些关于验证的约束条件。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now this is what I meant by grading like a reviewer. So if you've done a code review, you have a very strict reviewer, you're going to know they're not just going to think about the correctness of the solution. They're actually going to think about the style. They're going to think about maintainability. They're going to think of many different aspects. So what we do is we move from unit test based correctness to rubric base and these are generated from real commits. The rubrics also provide dense learning signal not just 01 signal and we penalize any sort of solution that is looking for a workaround. Right? So here is an example of a rubric where the criteria is you know here are the edge cases that need to be passed. This is an example of hygiene that needs to be maintained. These are constraints around verification that need to be satisfied.
</details>

现在，这不仅仅关乎评分所需的标准。我们还必须警惕评分裁判本身被钻空子，你真的需要加固裁判所处的环境。因此，我们考虑将评分裁判置于隔离的沙箱中。我们移除了 Git 历史和 Git remote，这样答案就不会泄露。测试集是保留不公开的。只有当智能体完成并提交了解决方案时，我们才会真正揭晓测试。我们遇到过这样的情况：智能体先假装说“嘿，我完成了”，等到测试显现后，它又去看测试并重新提交解决方案，对吧？所以你不希望发生这种事。此外，我们也希望在打分时做得更加细致。我们会提取完整的 diff，参考基准，并考量多个方面。不仅仅是正确性，我们还要看代码质量，还要看所做改动的范围。最后，我们要惩罚智能体的任何作弊企图，同时又注意不去管制它的思维链（thoughts）。任何作弊或破坏环境的行为都会受到非常严厉的强化学习负奖励。而所有这些都仅仅是根据模型采取的行动来检测的，而不是根据思维。在处理作弊方面，一个很好的例子是：在 Terminal Bench 4 上，Carbon 的作弊率为 0%。我们之前的模型作弊率要高得多，并且对抗性大约是 Argon 的五分之一。所以对于模型的每一代，我们都在努力让它更加对齐，让它不会试图去作弊。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now, it's not just about the rubrics needed for grading. We also need to be careful about the graders not being gamed and you really need to harden the environment that the grader is in. So, we think about putting the graders in a isolated sandbox. So we remove git history and git remote so the answers don't leak. The test sets are held out. So only when the agent is done and submits the solution do we actually reveal the test. We've had instances where the agent will fake it and say hey I I'm done and the tests come into play and then it'll look at the test and resubmit the solution. Right? So you don't want that. Um we also want to be much more detailed with our scoring. So we take the full diff and we look at the reference and we look at multiple aspects. It's not just the correctness. We also want to look at the code quality. We also want to look at the scope uh of the change that was done. And then finally, we want to penalize any sort of cheating attempt by the agent while being careful not to police the thoughts. Um so any you know cheating or hampering of the environment gets a very negative RL reward. Uh and all of this is detected just based on the actions that the model is taking, not the thoughts. Um so a good example of how we've handled cheating is you know carbon for instance has 0% cheating rate on terminal bench 4. Our previous models had a much higher cheating rate and it's about 5x less adversarial than argon. So every generation of the model we're trying to make it much more aligned so it's not trying to cheat.
</details>

### 模型诚实性与虚构抑制

现在最后，我想稍微谈谈模型行为，具体来说是模型诚实性。

智能体总是想表现得很有用，有时它们会假装有用。你在使用智能体时可能见过这种情况：有时它们声称测试已经通过，但显然并没有通过；它们宣布已完成，但显然没有完成；它们声称自己做了修改，但并没有做那些修改；或者它们会凭空捏造事实、API 等等；或者它们会给你一大篇包含大量数字的长篇大论，让你根本无法验证，对吧？我们经历过所有这些情况。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now finally I think uh I want to talk about model behavior a little bit and modeling honesty specifically.

Now agents they always want to be helpful and sometimes they fake helpfulness. You might have seen this in your usage of the agents. So sometimes they'll claim that tests have passed when they've obviously not passed. They'll declare done but obviously they're not done. They'll make edits that they'll claim to have made edits but they've not made those edits. Or they'll invent facts and APIs and whatnot. or they will give you a wall of text that has so many numbers that is going to be impossible for you to verify. Right? We've gone through all of these examples.
</details>

这是 Gemini 中捏造率的一个例子。在一次内部评估中，我们在那项困难的评测中一开始有 8% 的捏造率。在 Hydrogen 上，我们大幅将其削减到大约 3%，而在 Carbon 上，这一比例又减半，降到了大约 1.5%。但我们正努力将这个数字降至零。就像你绝不希望自己的智能体捏造任何东西一样，对吧？但它们之所以试图捏造，是因为它们想提供帮助。你真的必须下大功夫去对齐它们，让它们提供帮助，但不是通过弄虚作假的方式。

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Now, here is an example of fabrication rates in Gemini. Right? So, we on an internal eval started with 8% fabrication in that hard eval. We cut that down dramatically to about 3% for hydrogen and carbon is half of that is about 1.5%. But we're striving to get this down to zero. Like you don't want your agents fabricating anything, right? But they try to fabricate because they want to be helpful. And you really have to try hard to align them to be helpful but not by faking it.
</details>

我们如何减少捏造？不管你信不信，你必须清洗你的数据，对吧。所以我们过滤掉了大量训练轨迹，在这些轨迹中，不仅存在有意的捏造，也存在无意的捏造。其中有许多未得到充分支撑的主张，它们没有经过验证。我们以完整轨迹为依据（grounding）。如果智能体说“我已完成任务”并给出了最终回复，我们实际上会去查看那个回复，查看智能体在轨迹中为达成该最终解决方案所采取的所有动作，并审视所采取的步骤。因此，当智能体说“我完成了”并给你一份总结时，必须以所采取的实际动作为依据。并且我们希望奖励诚实。如果有难题，智能体更好的做法是说：“嘿，这是我的部分进展。这是一个难题。我在这里停下了。这是我的局部解。”而不是在最后一步弄虚作假，说：“嘿，我已经解决了这个问题。你看，搞定了。”

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
How do we reduce the fabrication? Believe it or not, you have to clean your data. Uh right. So we filter out lots of training trajectories where there is you know not just uh intended fabrication but unintended fabrication. There's lots of claims that are not fully supported. They're not verified. We ground in the full trajectory. So if the agent says I've completed the task and it has a final response, we actually look at that response and see all of the actions that the agent had taken in the trajectory to get to that final solution and we look at the steps that were taken. So you really need to when the agent says I'm done and gives you a summary that needs to be grounded in the actions that were taken. And we want to reward honesty. So if there's a hard problem, it's better for the agent to say, "Hey, this is my partial progress. It's a hard problem. I'm done here. This is my partial solution." Rather than faking the last step and saying, "Hey, I've solved the problem. You know, this is done."
</details>

### 以周为周期的快速迭代发布

最后，在几周内完成发布。那么我们为什么能够如此迅速地发布呢？

其中最大的原因，是我们在 Google 内部拥有一支超过 10 万名工程师的团队，他们将该模型用于实际任务。你们很多人可能知道 Google 采用的是单一代码库（monorepo）。什么是 monorepo？整家公司共用同一个代码库，对吧，我不知道该怎么衡量这个，但我想它可能是世界上最大的单一代码库之一。那是一个非常复杂的代码库，而我们拥有世界上最优秀的人才，使用着一个极其复杂的代码库来完成极其复杂的任务。因此，每次我们通过后训练得到新的候选模型时，我们实际上都会将该模型提供给我们的用户。我们通过多个不同的候选模型与他们进行盲测对决（blind battles）。我们让用户选出他们想要的最佳模型。我们也会运行 Elis，在底层将人员路由到实验性模型，并从中获取信号和反馈，然后把所有这些信号和阻力反馈到下一个候选模型的训练中。

所以这是一个帮助我们快速迭代、真正针对困难问题进行训练的循环，并且随着……

<details><summary>Original English</summary>
Finally, shipping in weeks. So how are we able to ship so rapidly and the biggest reason for that is our internal uh team of 100,000 plus engineers at Google using the model for realistic tasks. You know many of you might know Google is a monor repo and what is a monor repo? The entire company is one codebase right and I don't know how to measure this but I think it might be one of the largest monor repos in the world. Now that's a really complex uh code base and we have the best people in the world using a really complex codebase to do really complex tasks. So every time we have a new candidate from post training, we actually serve that model uh for our users. We run blind battles with them through multiple different candidates. We let users you know uh pick the best model that they want. We also run Elis where under the hood we're routing the routing people to experimental models and get signal and feedback from that and we take all of these signals and friction back into training the next candidate.

So this is a cycle that helps us you know rapidly iterate really train on hard problems and as
</details>

 the models get better the engineers are going to push the model in interesting ways.

关于构建软件的一切都将彻底改变。我想我们都感受到了，关于为了做软件而组建公司的方式、销售软件的方式、分发软件的方式，一切都将彻底改变。所有其他工作都将发生变化。那绝对是一个疯狂、疯狂的时刻。呃，当时我和我们团队的 Thorsten Ball 正好在法兰克福机场。呃，他写了那本关于编译器的书。认识我们团队 Thorsten 的人都知道。对，他是个很酷的家伙。呃，我们在法兰克福机场简直被震撼得说不出话来，我们深知一切都将改变。我们不知道这一切最终会走向何方，但我们就是想全速前进。我们只想迎头冲上去。因此，在 Sourcegraph 内部——那是一家 200 人的企业级软件公司，我是 CEO，我们做代码搜索——我们决定直接开始做这个，抛开其他所有事情，专心打造这个产品。不去参加销售拜访，不去开什么高管会议，统统都不参加。直接屏蔽掉所有的干扰。

而我当时所处的位置，我想很多人都会说：“哇，你看，我是公司里的软件开发人员，我想做出改变。我希望我的公司能更加积极拥抱 AI，但我做不到。”呃，你看，我是 CEO，理论上我可以改变任何事情。我可以调整团队。我可以宣布不需要提 PR 做代码审查，呃，不需要遵循我们过去的那套流程，但即便如此，这依然非常艰难。我们最终的做法是从公司带走了 20 个人，呃，依然在 Sourcegraph 内部成立了一个小团队，也就是 AMP 团队，呃，那就是我们的尝试。所以对于那些正在试图在公司内顶住压力推进变革、努力让环境对智能体更友好的人，我非常理解你们，我希望你们能从这里带走一些收获。呃，我知道这有多难，同时也对你们的 CEO 多一份理解。这对他们来说同样很艰难。这是一种非常困难的变化和转型。

但我们很清楚，有些事情一定会改变。这一切现在看来可能有点显而易见，但在 2025 年、2025 年初那阵子，关于“企业级就绪”的概念——即你把这件事做好，然后就能建一支销售团队拿去卖——我们知道这种模式将会彻底消亡。你必须站在最前沿。如果你尝到了点甜头，然后就开始逐条对照企业级清单去打勾以取悦普通企业买家，你的产品大概 3 个月内就会被淘汰，整个游戏规则不得不改变。除了让其他公司的员工真正爱上你的产品之外，其他一切都无所谓了；而 CEO 手头的预算是无限的，所有过去的常规逻辑都不适用了。这一点我们心里清楚。只是我们当时并不完全清楚呃我们会遇到哪些挑战。

You do this thing and then you can build a sales team and go sell it. We knew that that was just going to totally die off. You had to be on the frontier. If you had, you know, a taste of success and then you started doing all the enterprise checklist to make the median enterprise buyer happy, your product was going to go obsolete in like 3 months and the whole game had to change. Nothing mattered other than making it so that the people at these other companies loved your product and the CEO, the budget was infinite and all bets were off. So, we knew that. 呃，另外，在智能体能够构建产品或开发功能的这个世界里，你到底该如何思考差异化？我的意思是，直到今天依然有人问：“那为什么 AMP 更好？为什么，你看，Claude Code 比 Codex 或其他什么更好？”这么多人都在苦苦求索一个答案，希望其形式是：“你看，是因为这三项功能。”比如，呃，AMP 拥有 Orbs，这是非常优秀的云端智能体，而且我们有多人协作功能。但疯狂的地方在于，你们都知道如果我们能做到这些并且证明了它可行，那我们的任何竞争对手也同样能做到。既然如此，为什么还要去玩那种游戏呢？比如说，构建和销售软件到底意味着什么？如果你连一个月后它会变成什么样都不知道，你为什么还要去招聘销售团队？为什么还要按传统方式去做任何事情？

Um, also, you know, in this world where agents can build a product or build a feature, how do you even think about differentiation? And so many people are begging to have the answer in the form of, well, it's these three features. You know, uh, AMP has orbs, which are really good cloud agents, and we have multiplayer. 而且如果你甚至不知道该如何描述差异化，那就根本不存在可持续的差异化。呃，对于 AMP 来说，你看，大家最终都很喜欢它。呃，但你甚至可以看到，像呃 Tailwind 的作者 Adam Wathan，他很喜欢 AMP，但他说，你看，这属于那种你无法仅凭言语去理解的东西，呃，你看，在亲自使用它之前，你无法理解它到底好在哪里。你能想象拥有一个庞大的产品管理团队、设计团队、工程团队，他们却与实际客户如此脱节吗？如果这就是你推广产品最好的方式，整套公司架构运转起来真的会极其艰难。所以我们知道自己必须改变一切，我们对太多的事情进行了重新思考，因为可以说在无意之中，我们置身于一些非常奇怪的限制之下。

And if you don't even know how to describe differentiation, there's no sustainable differentiation. Um, for AMP, you know, people end up loving it. 我们决定将 AMP 剥离出来独立运作。所以对于那些在大公司里待过的人，比如一个小团队，呃，你看，你在公司里的一个小团队，你试图以不同的方式做事，你试图成为大初创公司里的内部创业团队，你可能也有过这样的幻想：干脆我们直接脱离出来创办一家新公司吧。而我们真的这么做了。我们在投资人和员工的利益上都处理得很周全，每个人在两家公司都有股份，但我们确实独立拆分出来了；而且在整整一年里，直到上周五，我们都不能招聘任何人，也不能授予期权。法律层面的拆分流程极其痛苦，我们被迫只能靠带出来的 21 个人勉力支撑。

<details>

We decided to spin out AMP.
